Abstract

Matter–Antimatter Pair Storm: An Emergent Statistical Framework for the Unification of Fundamental Forces

MAPS 统一框架 · 备忘录快照 · 2026-10-04 · 启发式统一框架（非已确立物理）

Draft v2.8 EN (English translation of the Chinese v2.8, incorporating responses to seven referee issues M1–M7) · 2026-08-24 · Heuristic unification framework (reference architecture, not established physics) Notation convention: 【Axiom】 = fundamental postulate; 【Assumption】 = physical assumption introduced by this framework; 【Derivation】 = derivation carried out within the framework; 【Citation】 = established physical/mathematical result; 【To be verified】 = conjecture requiring further verification. Term-strength convention (v2.2): Theorem = mathematically rigorous, framework-independent (including established results); Proposition = deduction valid under the framework axioms; Corollary = direct consequence of a proposition; Conjecture = unproved guess; Argument = heuristic/structural reasoning; 【Numerical】 = numerical verification result.

Abstract

This paper proposes a unified physical framework based on a single underlying mechanism, the matter–antimatter pair storm, providing an emergent statistical-mechanical account of the unification of the four fundamental forces (gravity, electromagnetism, strong and weak nuclear forces). The framework begins with four axioms: (i) the universe at its deepest level is an event flow of continuously meeting–annihilating–regenerating matter–antimatter pairs, isotropic, 50:50 balanced, at Planck-scale frequency; (ii) annihilation has an extremely small probability of "leakage," whose energy-scale-dependent form unifies the baryon asymmetry and dark energy; (iii) the arrow of time bifurcates into two manifolds that are mutual CPT mirrors, with time = frequency of annihilation events; (iv) space can be curled into time (the limit of black-hole space–time interchange).

On top of the axioms, we establish the following chain of arguments:

  1. Emergence of space–time: The event flow constructs a smooth Lorentzian manifold via causal-set sprinkling and the Malament recovery theorem, giving rigorous mathematical language to "manifold = statistical average of micro-variations"; the six-direction structure of the discrete grid spans 3+3 dimensional space–time (three spatial + three temporal), and observers live on a two-dimensional "time surface" (§3.3).

  2. Emergence of gravity (§4, core): mass-shielded flux produces a negative pressure difference, and the flux density decays according to the Boltzmann distribution ρ∝e^{−Φ/c²} of the gravitational potential (derived from ergodicity + detailed balance + chaotic propagation, Proposition 2.1), giving g₀₀ = −e^{−2Φ/c²} and hence PPN parameters γ=1, β=1, all passing experimental verdicts; the "micro-variation weight conservation" proposition fixes the spatial components; via the Jacobson thermodynamic path, the Einstein equation emerges as a horizon-thermodynamics identity. Black-hole entropy S = A/(4l_P²) acquires a complete argument chain: - Microstate counting (§4.8): flux-tube coherent blocks (1 nat per 4 Planck areas, k = e^{1/4} ≈ 1.284; raw states = 2^4 = 16 (4 in-plane connections × {+ branch, − branch}, reading B2, see §4.8)), with G, ℏ appearing as composites of the Planck area; - Packing proposition package (§4.10): geometric origin of the 1/4 coefficient — Theorem 1 (k≥4 lower bound, new proof) + Tammes regular tetrahedron + exact Voronoi partition + Proposition 6 (interior-space encoding priority, framework-internal deduction) — five occurrences of "4" cross-validated as one source; - Observer model (§4.9): observer = present-plane soliton (O1–O4), accessible information = mutual information I(R;Σ), holography = geometric necessity of observer limitation; "present-plane selection" (N1–N4) closed by vortex dynamics (4π phase locking) + spontaneous symmetry breaking; initial perturbation = ε leakage event (P1–P4, "necessary contingency"). - v1.21 completion of the gravity side: (f) isotropic exponential metric identification — Boltzmann g₀₀=−e^{−2x} + micro-variation weight conservation g_rr=e^{2x} + Axiom 1 isotropy ⟹ g_μν=diag(−e^{−2x}, e^{2x}, r²e^{2x}, r²sin²θ·e^{2x}) (the literature exponential metric), complete PPN parameter set γ=β=1, all others 0 (indistinguishable from GR at 1PN), 2PN g_rr bifurcation 33%, 3PN g₀₀ bifurcation 11% (B3); (g) shadow compatible with EHT — in the isotropic convention the framework shadow b_min=2e·GM/c²=5.436 GM/c² is compatible with EHT 5.2±0.3 (+4.55%), correcting v1.14's "strong-field conflict with EHT" (an artifact of the standard coordinate convention) — the framework's strong field is upgraded from "no metric commitment" to "geometric predictions compatible with observation"; (h) direction discretization — Planck-tube diameter 【Framework assumption: direction discretization】⟹ direction cluster k=4 (Wang Hong/Tammes) + regular-tetrahedron convex covering + statistical average yielding continuous directions ⟹ Bekenstein bound S≤A/(4l_P²) recovered from direction discretization, doubly closed with the k=e^{1/4} microstate counting ("=" directions) (B4); (i) observer mutual-information area law — bulk = surface projection ⟹ H(R|Σ)=0 ⟹ I(R;Σ)=H(Σ) ∝ A; the observer's raw 4 bits/plaquette (= 16 raw states from 4 directions × 4 states {+, −, annihilating, vacuum}) compressed coherently by 16 ln 2 = 11.0904 to 1 nat per coherent block ⟹ entropy density 1/4 nat/plaquette (= 0.3607 bit/plaquette) ⟹ S/k_B = A/(4l_P²) (B5).

  3. Gauge-group structure: The Standard Model gauge group SU(3)×SU(2)×U(1) corresponds to the representation-theoretic structure of "pairing orders" n=3,2,1 (v2.5: uniqueness via three-criterion structural screening — d_min matching / same-sign pairing order / third-order antisymmetric tensor, 10 candidates eliminated one by one, §5.2); color confinement acquires its mathematical root in the third-order antisymmetric tensor; the three fermion generations correspond to three winding modes of the vortex; the multi-representation unified field gives the four forces as a partial-wave decomposition of flux-field fluctuations. v1.19 gauge-side emergence arguments (§5.6–5.8): (a) quantum-double algebra proposition package — the framework's three event-flow operations ↔ Hopf structure, non-Abelian gauge structure emerging exactly from discrete algebra (Kitaev theorem, Z₂/Z₃/S₃ numerical verification); (b) framework Hamiltonian H_F = −JΣA_v − KΣB_p — [H_F,A_v]=[H_F,B_p]=0 proves gauge symmetry is a dynamical symmetry (emergent), 2+1D positive energy gap (topological order) is a low-dimensional rigorous realization of the mass gap, torus degeneracy = |G|²; (c) Wen string-net 3+1D route — quantum lines = string-net edges (Framework assumption: cosmic strings = dynamically emergent network), four forces = emergent channels of string-net order (EM→U(1) string-net photon, gravity→string-net emergent spin-2); (f) transition-point localization (P0-3 open-boundary FSS → v1.23 torus refinement) — order parameter m=⟨X⟩ crossing: open-boundary FSS κ_c≈0.25±0.05 (v1.22); torus (periodic boundary) refinement: half-polarization point κ*≈0.20±0.01 (2×2/2×3/2×4/3×3 size-independent, boundary pollution removed); criterion correction — the m=0.5 crossing is a half-polarization point, not the transition point (torus measurement m(h=0.32)≈0.84), true transition κ_c≈0.25–0.32, consistent with the literature X-field (σ^x field) value h_xc≈0.32 (note: h_c=0.328 is the σ^z-field value, mixed up in P0-3, corrected in v1.23) — topological-order→confinement transition confirmed, confinement phase diagram complete (§5.7).

  4. Foundations of quantum mechanics: The classical event flow cannot produce Bell violation; the framework must be non-commutativized — the event flow is upgraded to an operator algebra, with CHSH = 2√2 and complementarity V²+I²≤1 proven; the positioning is 't Hooft-like: providing an ontological motivation ("event flow does not commute") for the Hilbert-space structure of quantum mechanics.

  5. The framework yields a number of testable predictions, including: black-hole strong-field prediction package (B1–B5) (extreme-Kerr Hawking-radiation angular distribution deviating from axisymmetry, primordial black-hole entropy volume correction ~10⁻⁸, Hawking-radiation–infalling-matter entanglement, ER-bridge packing bound, non-thermal correction 1/S), EMRI precession-difference verdict (S-E1–S-E4: after v1.35 correction, ΔlnL criterion 27σ/28σ (robust with a-φ0 fully free, σ_δ=1.6×10⁻⁶)), FQH graviton-mode ε term (S-E5; closed in rounds 53–54: ε was mislabelled 10⁻²⁰ (actually O(1) at FQH scales), Γ_flux is undefined, and the identification "FQH graviton mode = framework string-net mode" has no valid basis (string-net has no chirality + 10³⁰ apart) ⟹ directional claim, no quantitative content), accretion-disk firewall non-thermal correction (S4, primordial black-hole window), void–mirror-branch gravitational-projection correlation (void-CPT-κ anomaly), constant deviation of dark energy w=−1+4ε/3, SU(3) β-function staircase jump, ln 2 factor in the entanglement-entropy area law.

Honesty statement: This framework is a heuristic unification framework, not established physics. All 【Derivation】s in this paper are internally consistent arguments within the framework, and no 【To be verified】 item may be used as a basis for derivation. The v1.0 strong-field metric extrapolation prediction (g₀₀=−e^{−2x} neutron-star deviation) has been formally withdrawn (beyond the domain of validity, §4.6); the framework's strong-field content is the seven-loop mechanism chain + isotropic exponential metric (shadow compatible with EHT, §4.5b/§4.6). Remaining major open problems: complete emergence of the non-Abelian component (4D), concrete values of J/K coupling, string-net emergent U(1) continuum limit and spin 2 (3+1D) (§10.2).

1 Introduction

1.1 Motivation and background

The two pillars of modern physics — general relativity (GR) and quantum field theory (QFT) — precisely describe gravity and the other three fundamental forces respectively, yet their unification (quantum gravity) is the deepest unsolved problem of physics. The Standard Model unifies strong, weak and electromagnetic forces via the SU(3)×SU(2)×U(1) gauge group; GR describes gravity via Riemannian geometry. However:

  • Gravity is non-renormalizable and lacks a complete quantum description;
  • The Standard Model contains 19–26 free parameters (hierarchy problem, cosmological-constant problem, strong-CP problem);
  • The relative strengths of the four forces (1 : 10⁻² : 10⁻⁶ : 10⁻³⁸) lack a unified explanation;
  • Candidate paths such as grand unification (GUT), string theory, and loop quantum gravity have all lacked experimental support.

This framework proposes a different unification strategy: instead of postulating a larger symmetry group or extra dimensions, it seeks a single underlying statistical mechanism (the event flow) such that the four forces become emergent modes of the same mechanism at different scales/configurations. This strategy is analogous to the relation of statistical mechanics to thermodynamics: GR is the geometric description, and the framework's statistical-fluid description is its effective theory.

1.2 Core concept: the matter–antimatter pair storm

【Axiom 1】The universe at its deepest level is an event flow of continuously meeting–annihilating–regenerating matter–antimatter pairs, isotropic, 50:50 balanced, at Planck-scale frequency (ν ~ t_P⁻¹ ≈ 10⁴³ s⁻¹). Each encounter is a "micro-variation"; the space–time manifold = statistical average of micro-variations ("the manifold varies infinitely finely").

The four fundamental forces = four modes of the same storm:

Force Mode Mechanism
Strong Shielding harbor Nearby particles share a low-pressure cavity (intuition for color confinement)
Weak Draft/wind Extremely short-range pipe coupling (chirality, W/Z masses)
Electromagnetic Selective coupling Long-range flux-pressure gradient (cavity model)
Gravity Flux negative pressure Long-range, extremely weak flux-occlusion deficit

Unification = disappearance of mode-boundary distances: the three energy scales — electroweak unification (100 GeV), GUT (10¹⁵ GeV), Planck (10¹⁹ GeV) — are a ladder on which mode boundaries vanish one by one; symmetry breaking = mode boundaries re-emerge as flux density decreases.

1.3 Paper structure

§2 establishes the four axioms and fundamental objects; §3 argues the emergent generation of space–time (causal sets, time surface, 3+3 dimensions); §4 is the core chapter — the complete emergence argument for gravity (Boltzmann distribution, PPN verdicts, microstate counting, thermal-equilibrium propositions, seven-loop strong-field mechanism, packing proposition package, observer model O1–O4, present-plane selection N1–N4, initial-perturbation origin P1–P4); §5 gauge-group structure; §6 dynamics (least action); §7 foundations of quantum mechanics; §8 cosmology; §9 testable predictions; §10 conclusions and open problems.

2 Axioms and Basic Postulates

2.1 The four axioms

【Axiom 1 (matter–antimatter pair storm)】: The universe at its deepest level is an event flow of continuously meeting–annihilating–regenerating matter–antimatter pairs, isotropic, 50:50 balanced, at Planck-scale frequency; the manifold = statistical average of micro-variations.

【Axiom 2 (leakage mechanism)】: Each annihilation has a small probability ε of binding failure; the positive fraction leaks into the positive-time-arrow branch, the anti fraction into the mirror branch. ε is energy-scale dependent: ε(E) = ε₀(M_Pl/E)^β, β ≈ 0.615.

【Axiom 3 (bifurcation of the time arrow)】: The two branches are mutual CPT mirrors; time = encounter/annihilation frequency, defined by the generation rate of the overlap integral τ(x) = ∫ψ̄ψ d⁴x.

【Axiom 4 (space curled into time)】: Space can be curled into time; the space–time interchange inside a black hole = space curled to its limit (radial direction curled into the frame-dragging direction).

2.2 Fundamental objects and definitions

  • Micro-variation weight current J^μ【Derivation】: the conserved current of the annihilation event flow (∇_μ J^μ = 0, for ε ≪ 1). Micro-variation weight W[Σ] = ∫_Σ J^μ dΣ_μ.
  • Flux density ρ_pair(x): local annihilation-event rate density, determining the flow rate of time.
  • Quantum line: the correlation channel between the two branches established by leakage events of common origin (the underlying ontology of ER=EPR).
  • Plaquette: the fundamental cell constituting the manifold (six-directional connections, spanning three planes).

2.3 Hierarchical structure of mechanics

Event flow (Planck noise)
  ├── Statistical average ⟹ manifold / space–time
  ├── Solitonization ⟹ particles (stable localized structures)
  ├── Pressure resistance ⟹ mass / inertia (equivalence principle = two sides of the same coupling)
  ├── Flux response ⟹ four-force modes ⟹ rising energy scale ⟹ unification / breaking
  └── Leakage ε ⟹ time arrow / baryon asymmetry / dark energy

3 Emergence of Space–Time

3.1 Axiomatization via causal sets

【Citation + Derivation】 Formalize the Axiom-1 event flow as a causal set: an event set C with partial order ≺, satisfying non-reflexivity, transitivity, and local finiteness. Each encounter–annihilation ∈ C; "can influence each other" = ≺. Isotropy corresponds to Poisson sprinkling: random points placed on a smooth Lorentzian manifold (M,g) as a Poisson process with density ρ = l_P⁻⁴.

Malament recovery theorem【Citation】: If (M,g) and (M′,g′) are both past/future-distinguishing manifolds with isomorphic causal orders, then M ≅ M′ and g′ = Ω²g — the causal structure uniquely determines the conformal class of the metric. The conformal factor is fixed by event counting:

gμν(x) = Ω2(x)g0μν(x), Ω4(x) = dN(x)ρ dV0(x)

Three-step proposition chain for "manifold micro-variation"【Derivation】: 1. Poisson sprinkling (law of large numbers) → causal partial order; 2. Malament recovery theorem → conformal class of metric; 3. Counting density → conformal factor Ω.

3.2 Time = event frequency

【Derivation】 For a static metric ds² = g₀₀ dt² + h_ij dx^i dx^j, an ideal clock = event counter. Events occur uniformly at Planck frequency f₀ in proper time; the coordinate-time event rate f(x):

g00(x) = -(f(x)f0)2, fBfA = √g00(B)g00(A) ≈ 1 + ΦB - ΦAc2

The weak field gives gravitational redshift — frequency gradient corresponds one-to-one to the g₀₀ gradient.

3.3 3+3 dimensional discrete space–time and the time surface

【Derivation】 The six directions of the plaquette (front/back, left/right, up/down) span three coordinate axes and three planes (xy/xz/yz), corresponding respectively to past/present/future — three temporal dimensions. We perceive only one temporal dimension because the other two are curled into the normal directions of the spatial planes.

Corollary (holography = geometric necessity of the time surface)【Derivation, complete proposition chain in §4.9】: - Observer = local soliton on the present plane (O1); accessible information = projection onto the time surface = mutual information I(R;Σ) (O2); - Bulk information is redundant, surface information is primary; information capacity ∝ surface area (O4); - The observer's uniform prior is derived from observer geometry (O3) — no information channel along external spatial directions.

Why the "present plane" is selected【Derivation, proposition chain in §4.9】: the vortex must lock its rotation axis (N1) and exactly one plane (N2); the three planes are equivalent (N3, "present" = definition of the selected plane); the initial perturbation (= ε leakage event, P1–P4) breaks the SO(3) degeneracy (N4).

3.4 Open problems of the continuum limit

【To be verified】 ① prove "event set = Poisson sprinkling" from annihilation dynamics; ② convergence of the continuum limit (not fully resolved in standard causal-set theory either); ③ proof of the postulate "each event = 1 proper-time element".

4 Emergence of Gravity (Core Chapter)

4.1 Flux occlusion → gravitational potential

【Derivation】 An object of mass M "swallows" flux, producing a steady-state deficit. In source-free regions the flux deficit satisfies ∇²(δρ) = 0, with spherically symmetric solution δρ ∝ 1/r; matching the Newtonian potential via the time component:

ρpair(r)ρpair(∞) = 1 - GMc2 r (first order)

Weak-field numerical check: at the solar surface GM/c²R = 2.1×10⁻⁶ ≪ 1 ✓.

Factor-2 explanation (v1.12): The flux-deficit coefficient is taken as 1 (GM/c²r, same form as the Newtonian potential Φ/c²), not 2GM/c²r — the "2" in g₀₀ comes from the squared structure g₀₀ = −(ρ/ρ∞)² (§4.3; Axiom 3: time = event frequency ⟹ √(−g₀₀)=ρ/ρ∞). That is: - Flux deficit ρ∞−ρ_ss = ρ∞·GM/(c²r): first-order coefficient 1 (= Φ/c²); - g₀₀ = −(1−GM/(c²r))² = −(1−2GM/(c²r)+…): first-order coefficient 2 (consistent with GR, β_ppn=1 self-consistent). This also fixes the flux-vanishing radius of the finite-R steady state (v1.11) as R = GM/c² (not the Schwarzschild 2GM/c²). Numerical verification: R=GM/c² agrees with the Boltzmann weak field (deviation <0.6% for r≥10), while R=2GM/c² deviates >11%; the squared g₀₀ structure differs from the GR weak field by 1.0% at r=10 and 0.01% at r=100.

Strong-field bifurcation verdict (v1.13)【Proposition】: the PPN β parameters of the two candidate paths bifurcate — the Boltzmann g₀₀=−e^{−2Φ/c²} expansion has β=1 (passes observation 1±3×10⁻⁴), while the squared steady-state g₀₀=−(1−R/r)² expansion has β=1/2 (excluded at 1600σ). ⟹ The Boltzmann path is the correct description from weak to intermediate field; the squared steady state is only a first-order approximation (still valid as a flux profile, but not an exact g₀₀ mapping). The v1.12 horizon prediction r=GM/c² is withdrawn (it was based on the squared steady state with g₀₀=0, excluded by the β verdict). Framework strong field: the Boltzmann factor never vanishes ⟹ no g₀₀=0 geometric horizon; the horizon = storm overload / flux-vanishing equipotential surface (physical mechanism, framework assumption) — consistent with v1.1's "strong field undefined" and the seven-loop mechanism.

4.2 Boltzmann distribution (flux-field thermal equilibrium)

【Derivation + thermal-equilibrium proof】 The flux density decays with the Boltzmann weight of the gravitational potential:

ρ(r)ρ∞ = e-Φ(r)/c2 = 1 - x + 12 x2 - ⋯, x = GMc2 r

Thermal-equilibrium proposition (six-step chain)【Derivation】: 1. The event flow is a stationary random process (SDE dX_t = b dt + σ dW); 2. Ergodicity (Birkhoff–Khinchin theorem): time average = ensemble average — the B–K theorem presupposes metric transitivity, so this is a dynamical assumption, not a theorem output; numerically verified in the v2.6 supplement below (measured 0.00%); 3. Microscopic reversibility (annihilation–regeneration CPT symmetry); 4. Chaotic propagation (Boltzmann–Grad limit, linking Hilbert's sixth problem / the Deng–Hani–Ma hard-sphere chain) ⟹ Boltzmann equation; 5. Detailed balance ⟹ Boltzmann distribution ρ ∝ e^{−E/kT}; 6. kT = mc² (relativistic limit) ⟹ ρ ∝ e^{−Φ/c²}.

Numerical simulation verification【Derivation】: detailed-balance Markov chain converges to the Boltzmann distribution (deviation 2.12%).

【v2.9 audit correction: replacing the ergodicity evidence and recording the failed cases】【Numerical】: re-running _m1_boltzmann.py and the new _m1_ergodicity_proper.py (EG-1…EG-3): - ① The original evidence is not an ergodicity test: the reported "ergodicity (first/second-half time averages differ <0.2%)" measures 0.00%, but a control experiment (collisions switched off during sampling, i.e. manifestly non-ergodic) also gives 0.00% ⟹ the indicator is completely insensitive to non-ergodicity. It actually tests stationarity/absence of drift of the temperature, and since T is fixed by energy conservation it is nearly identically zero. It should be relabelled as evidence for item 1 (stationarity). - ② A genuine Birkhoff test is used instead (observable O = v²; compare the spread of the per-particle time averages ⟨O_i⟩_t as a function of sample number n_t; for the 2D Maxwellian the single-sample relative spread is 1): in the original setting the spread decreases monotonically, 0.971 → 0.848 → 0.553 → 0.284 → 0.183 (n_t = 25/100/400/1600/4000), whereas the frozen control stays at 1.012 and does not decrease with n_t. ⟹ Ergodicity holds (the ∝1/√n_t trend is clear; the decrease is slower than the ideal value because of sampling correlations). - ⟹ The conclusion is right, the original evidence was wrong: item 2's conclusion is retained, with the Birkhoff test above as its evidence.

Citations must state the layer.

⟹ The three do not conflict, because they do not ask the same question: L1 is local equilibration of velocities (fast, ~4 t_P); L2 is orbital closure of configurations (not "not yet thermalized" but "cannot get there"); L3 is displacement (slow). ⟹ Round 40's "equipartition holds instantaneously / no thermalization needed" rests exactly on the timescale separation between L1 and L3 (τ_relax ≈ 4 t_P versus t_H, a ratio of ~10⁶⁰, §4.2's two timescales). ⚠️ Writing rule: "ergodicity holds" must say L1 (velocity space); "non-ergodic" must say L2 (configuration space); the "everything" reading (round 57, reading (c)) refers to L2. ⚠️ One candidate needing clarification (found this round): candidate-pool item 14 records the third entry as "round 21's 47 μm spatial transport" and calls it a kind of "ergodicity", but a full-text search shows the word "ergodic" is never used at the 47 μm passage (R_mix is a diffusion scaling, not an ergodicity criterion) ⟹ this note lists it separately as L3 and renames it, so as not to perpetuate the inaccurate phrase "three kinds of ergodicity". - ③ Criterion calibration (important correction: the "run A fails" note in the previous version was a criterion artifact): this weighted-deviation metric has a sample-size-dependent statistical noise floor (obtained by sampling from the exact Maxwellian with identical bins/weights, averaged over 400 trials): N=256 → 24.3%±4.9%, N=500 → 16.6%, N=512 → 16.5%, N=1000 → 11.4%, N=1024 → 11.4%, N=2000 → 8.0%, N=8000 → 3.9%. ⟹ the absolute criterion "<10%" is unusable for N ≲ 1600 (the floor itself is ≥10%). Re-auditing the three runs against the floor: · run A (deterministic hard disks, N=256) measures 22.74% = 0.94× the floor (within 1σ) ⟹ indistinguishable from a perfectly correct Maxwellian — there is no "not fully converged"; moreover quadrupling the number of steps (20000) leaves the deviation at 28.25% (1.16× floor), i.e. it does not decrease ⟹ "insufficient relaxation" is further excluded, and the residual can be attributed to the 2D long-time tail (already honestly noted in the draft); · run C: v0=2.0/N=1000 gives 13.6% = 1.19× the N=1000 floor 11.4% (1.1σ); v0=1.0/N=500 gives 20.7% = 1.25× the N=500 floor 16.6% (1.4σ) ⟹ both at the noise level, not physical failures; · run B (event-flow DSMC, N=2000): distribution 7.02% = 0.88× the 8.0% floor, together with temperature 0.00%, ΔH = −2.266 and the Birkhoff test ⟹ fully passing. ⟹ The criterion should be changed to the relative form "measured / noise floor ≲ 1.5"; the original absolute criterion is only ~1.4σ above the floor even at N=2000, so its discriminating power is limited. The core support for this proposition chain comes from run B.

Important limitation【To be verified】: the Boltzmann distribution of a pure 1/r potential diverges (r→0) — gravitational systems have no global thermal equilibrium (consistent with standard statistical mechanics); e^{−Φ/c²} is valid only locally / in the weak field / with cutoff, and its weak-field expansion agrees with §4.1.

4.4 Micro-variation weight conservation proposition (spatial components)

【Proposition (micro-variation weight conservation ⇒ γ=1)】: If the annihilation event flow satisfies conservation (Axiom 1′) and local isotropy (Axiom 1), then in a static spherically symmetric weak field g_rr = −1/g₀₀ = (1−2GM/c²r)⁻¹, hence PPN γ = 1.

Proof (three lines): ① local inertial-frame isotropy ⇒ the temporal weight √(−g₀₀) and radial weight √(g_rr) are complementary (product = 1); ② lemma: √(−g₀₀) = f(r)/f₀ = e^{−x}; ③ substitution ⇒ γ=1. ∎

4.5 PPN β=1 (second-order term)

【Derivation】 The second-order coefficient of the Boltzmann distribution is a = +1/2:

g00 = -e-2x = -(1 - 2x + 2β x2 + ⋯) ⟹ βPPN = 1

Numerical verification: PPN expansion comparison (Will convention g₀₀ = −(1−2x+2βx²)) ⟹ β = a + 1/2 = 1 ✓.

gμν = diag(-e-2x, e2x, r2 e2x, r2sin2θ e2x), x = GMc2 r

- F1′ (exact diffusion-equation solution): random-walk continuum limit ⟹ diffusion equation ∂ρ/∂t = D∂²ρ/∂x² (D = l_P·c/2) ⟹ horizon boundary ρ(0,t)=0 ⟹ erf solution ρ=ρ∞·erf(x/√(4Dt)) ⟹ recovery time t_rec=R/c ⟹ δ = √2·√(R·l_P) (exact 1D coefficient; zero numerical deviation); - F1″ (3D spherically symmetric exact solution): the u = rρ transformation exactly absorbs the 2/r term ⟹ 3D spherical symmetry reduces to 1D diffusion ⟹ mirror-method Green's function ⟹ closed-form solution ρ = ρ∞·[R·erf((r−R)/√(4Dt)) + (r−R)]/r (horizon ρ(R)=0, far field ρ→ρ∞, x≪R reduces to 1D — all three limits pass) ⟹ characteristic width δ = √2·√(R·l_P), half-value thickness δ₁/₂ = 0.6745·√(R·l_P); the validity of the 1D approximation is rigorously proven (geometric correction O(ε)=√(2l_P/R) ~ 10⁻¹⁹); dimensionless diffusion constant D_eff = 1/4 analytic identity; numerical verification: finite-difference deviation 0.0024%, half-value-point deviation 6×10⁻⁶; - F1‴ (finite-R exact solution): introducing an outer boundary L (cosmological horizon / nearby body) ⟹ separation of variables, spherical-Bessel series u = ρ∞(r−R)L/(L−R) + Σcₙ sin(kₙ(r−R))e^{−Dkₙ²t} (kₙ=nπ/(L−R)) ⟹ all numerical checks pass (convergence N≥200, initial/steady limits exact) ⟹ half-infinite approximation error quantified: deviation <0.01% for L/R≥1.1 (only failure = extremely close binary merger); important physical finding: the steady-state solution ρ_ss(r) = ρ∞(1−R/r) — the flux deficit ∝ 1/r, of the same form as the Newtonian potential (P0 derived the 1/r² force from solid-angle integration; this batch independently derives the 1/r potential from the diffusion steady state — two paths converge, independently supporting "gravity = flux occlusion"); - F2 (mode energy scale): δ=l_P ⟹ scale ~ E_P (AMPS risk); δ=√(R·l_P) ⟹ scale ~ E_P·√(l_P/R) (solar black hole 7.4×10⁻²⁰ E_P, genuinely gentle); - F3 (observational compatibility): tidal forces of both thicknesses are imperceptible (equivalence principle preserved); QNM correction δ/R ~ 10⁻¹⁹ unmeasurable — the verdict must come from theoretical mechanism; - F4 (emission-layer prediction): firewall = Hawking-radiation emission layer【Framework assumption】⟹ non-thermal correction ~ √(l_P/R) — primordial black holes ~10⁻⁹~10⁻⁴ measurable window (complementary to B5). - 【Honest caveat】 the early record "primordial black hole R~10⁸ l_P" differs from the standard formula R_s(10¹¹kg)≈9×10¹⁸ l_P by ~10¹¹ (likely a typographical error; the standard formula is adopted).

4.7 Emergence of the Einstein equation (Jacobson path)

【Citation + Derivation】 Rindler horizon + entropy area law (§4.8) + Clausius relation δQ = T dS ⟹ the Einstein equation emerges as a horizon-thermodynamics identity:

Gμν = 8π G Tμν

Framework connection: entropy area law (§4.8), temperature = flux gradient (Unruh), energy flow = flux occlusion, cutoff = Planck (naturally from Axiom 1). 【To be verified】 full closure still requires a rigorous dynamical derivation "event flow → entropy area law".

4.8 Black-hole entropy S = A/4: flux-tube coherent-block microstate counting

【Microstate-counting proposition】: the horizon = flux-saturated surface = dense packing of flux-tube endpoints. The surface has N = A/l_P² plaquettes; every 4 plaquettes form 1 coherent unit (Clifford 4 components: two time directions × two chirality directions), 1 nat per unit:

SkB = A4lP2 (nats) = A4lP2ln 2 (bits), k = e1/4 ≈ 1.284

Numerical verification: c·t_P = l_P (1 event per plaquette per Planck time); solar-mass black hole S = 1.05×10⁷⁷ ✓.

Mechanism of appearance of G, ℏ【Derivation】: S = A·c³/(4Gℏ) — G, ℏ appear as composites of the Planck area l_P² = Gℏ/c³ (not fundamental constants; derived quantities of the event-flow minimal scale).

Candidate comparison: Boltzmann factor (✗), LQG area spectrum (different origin, but same ln 2), Bekenstein 1-photon argument (weak bound 8π, ✗) — the flux-tube coherent-block model is the unique exact match. The geometric origin of the 1/4 coefficient (Kakeya packing, k≥4 lower-bound theorem) is in §4.10. 【To be verified】 the dynamical origin of k=e^{1/4} still requires "flux–coupling-mode counting".

【v2.9 correction: units and k (rounds 15/19/20)】 (every "1 bit/block" and "k=2^{1/4}" appearing in the v2.8 supplement below is superseded by this): - Unit correction (adjudicated by the Hawking temperature): both ends agree on 1 nat per 4l_P², not 1 bit. Taking 1 bit would give a solar-mass black hole temperature of 8.8995×10⁻⁸ K, i.e. +44.27% off the standard Hawking value; 1 nat gives 6.16868×10⁻⁸ K (deviation <10⁻⁹). ⟹ S/k_B = A/(4l_P²) (nats), i.e. entropy density 1/4 nat/plaquette = 0.3607 bit/plaquette. The original formula S = (A/4l_P²)ln2 (bits) = A/4l_P² (nats) differs between its two sides by one ln 2 and is corrected here. - Correction of k: k = 2^{1/4} = 1.1892 corresponds to "1/4 bit/plaquette" (ln k = 0.1733 nat); the corrected value is k = e^{1/4} = 1.2840 (ln k = 0.25 nat). The two differ by only 8% in value but by 44% in entropy (= 1/ln2) — the signature of mixing bits and nats. - Origin of the substrate state count (new in v2.9: turning an input of B5 into an output): inverting B5's "raw readable 4 bits/plaquette" for the number q of internal states per site, using the direction cluster k=4 already established in §4: q=2 → 3.000 bits, q=3 → 3.585, q=4 → 4.000 (unique), q=5 → 4.322. ⟹ the four internal states are exactly {+, −, annihilating, vacuum}, and the raw count is 4 directions × 4 states = 16 = 4 bits/plaquette (previously an assumption, now derived).

【Round-84 synchronisation note (C-35)】 The "q = 4 → 4.000 (unique)" above refers to uniqueness under inversion of B5's target "raw readable 4 bits/plaquette"; the separate claim "q = 4 is the unique solution" was withdrawn in round 32 (the reading verdict adopts B2: four in-plane connections each carrying a positive/negative branch ⟹ 2^4 = 16) — see the round-32 block below. ⚠️ This chain is moreover a consistency construction (inverted from 16 states and 1 nat/block) and therefore has no predictive power for S = A/4 (established in round 39). 【v2.9 correction (round 31: downstream re-check of the T-6 verdict)】 The line above assigns the "4 directions" to the direction cluster k = 4 of §4.10 (Tammes / regular tetrahedron, the interior-space layer). Rounds 30–31 show the correct attribution is the "four in-plane directions" of §4.9 Lemma 1 — the connection directions inside the observer's present plane. The exterior-space lattice coordination is 6 (simple cubic, three axes ±), of which 4 lie in the plane and 2 are curled away. Two independent reasons: (i) under reading A (direction count k times internal-state count q), k·q = 16 has no integer solution at k = 6 (16/6 = 2.667); (ii) under reading B (one binary connected/not-connected flag per direction), 2^k = 16 ⟹ k = 4, while k = 6 gives 64 states. ⟹ Both readings force the direction count to be 4; the exterior-space coordination 6 cannot be used here. The numerical conclusion "16 = 4 × 4" and the compression factor 16 ln 2 = 11.0904 are unchanged. ⚠️ Newly registered ambiguity A/B: reading A (4 directions × 4 states = 16, the original reading of this item) and reading B (4 binary connection flags = 2⁴ = 16, the literal reading of the source text 1651 "connection status in the four directions") agree numerically but differ structurally; they cannot be distinguished at present and are both kept as candidates. Discriminating clue (not pursued): under reading B, "vacuum" = no connection in that direction (inside the flags); under reading A, "vacuum" is an internal state on a par with the directions. 【v2.9 correction (round 32: adjudicating readings A / B)】 The A/B ambiguity registered above is resolved in favour of reading B2: 4 in-plane connections, each carrying the + or − branch ⟹ 2^4 = 16 = 4 bits/plaquette, rather than "1-of-4 direction × 4 internal states". Grounds: in the scoring matrix over five constraints, B2 is the only model that passes all of them — C-a raw readable 4 bits ✓; C-b full occupancy f = 1 (no empty slot, so "vacuum" carries no weight) ✓; C-c "annihilating" is a zero-measure in-tick transient ✓; C-d direction count = 4 (the four in-plane directions of §4.9 Lemma 1) ✓; C-e A1's 50:50 sits directly on each slot ✓. Reading A fails: it gives "vacuum" and "annihilating" the same weight as "+/−", contradicting C-b / C-c; removing the two leaves 4 × 2 = 8 states = 3 bits, inconsistent with 4 bits/plaquette — and if the compression factor 11.0904 is kept regardless, the Hawking temperature is off by +33.33% (compare −12.76% for N = 24). ⟹ 16 states, 4 bits, 0.25 nat/plaquette and the compression factor 16 ln 2 = 11.0904 are all unchanged; only the way 16 is counted changes, and B2 makes direct use of A1's 50:50 (which reading A did not). ⚠️ Cost: of {+, −, annihilating, vacuum} only + and − enter the entropy count; the four states demote to an algebraic state space with two weighted states ⟹ the round-19 claim "q = 4 internal states is the unique solution" is withdrawn. 【v2.9 addendum (round 33: the weight-w scan — a quantitative test of B2's soft spot)】 Adopting B2 above left one soft spot: whether C-b / C-c ("vacuum / annihilating carry no weight") amount to carrying a conclusion of the substrate-occupancy dynamics across layers into the plaquette state count. This round introduces the weight w explicitly and scans it; the conclusion is that B2 needs no such transfer: · Slot model (4 in-plane connections, each slot ∈ {+, −, empty}, empty weight w): H_S(w) = 4[h₂(w) + (1−w)ln 2]; requiring H_S = ln 16 (BH normalisation) leaves only w = 0 and w = 0.7729, the latter meaning 77.3% of the in-plane connections are empty, which conflicts with C-b (full occupancy f = 1) and is excluded. ⟹ w = 0 is solved for by BH normalisation together with full occupancy; it is not an imported assumption. · Contrast: model A (1-of-4 direction × four states) has its w pinned by BH at 0.500, while C-b / C-c require 0 — a gap of 0.5. Even demanding only a Hawking-temperature deviation ≤ 1% requires w ≥ 0.3833 (≤ 5% requires w ≥ 0.2489). ⟹ Model A is caught between two constraints, not merely slightly worse. ⟹ By-product: B2's "every in-plane connection always carries flux" now has a provenance (BH normalisation + full occupancy) instead of being an unsupported construct; the chain is: full occupancy ⟹ every site occupied ⟹ every bond carries flux ⟹ all 4 in-plane connections live, and then A1's 50:50 assigns + or − branch to each ⟹ 2^4 = 16. ⚠️ Still open: whether the slot model's empty-slot rate w and the substrate occupancy f are the same quantity has not been argued independently (assumed identical here); w = 0 is an exact BH requirement, so if the compression factor is ever re-derived this constraint moves. 【v2.9 addendum (round 34: the identity of w and f — closing the B2 loop)】 Round 33 left one soft spot: whether the empty-slot rate w and the substrate occupancy f are the same quantity (it was assumed that w = 1 − f). This round concludes: · They are not the same quantity: a plaquette's "in-plane connection" is a bond between sites, and a bond carries flux ⟺ both endpoints are occupied; for independent occupancy, w = 1 − f² (direct Monte-Carlo check: measured bond-survival rates at f = 0.9 / 0.8 / 0.6 / 0.5 agree with f² to within 2.3%). The round-33 default w = 1 − f misses a square and is wrong; but since f = 1 ⟹ w = 0 under either form, the conclusion is unchanged. · No identity assumption is needed: the framework's permutation dynamics is a bijection ⟹ every site always has exactly one 诸元 ⟹ f ≡ 1 identically, tick by tick (numerics: 0 non-permutation ticks in 1000, per-site occupation count identically 1) ⟹ w ≡ 0 identically, not merely with probability zero. ⟹ B2's "no empty slot" comes neither from a cross-layer transfer nor from site independence; it is a direct consequence of the permutation bijection. · The independent-Poisson substrate is excluded a second time: f* = 1/2 ⟹ w = 0.75 ⟹ Hawking-temperature deviation −5.77% (the first ground was round 20's Δc/c = 26%; the two are unrelated and cross-validate each other). · Solving the two constraints jointly, H_S(1 − f²) = ln 16 gives f = 1 or f = 0.4765; the latter leaves 52.3% of sites empty and conflicts with C-1 ⟹ excluded. ⟹ B2 closes the loop: full occupancy (permutation bijection) ⟹ every bond carries flux ⟹ all 4 in-plane connections live ⟹ A1's 50:50 assigns +/− to each ⟹ 2^4 = 16 ⟹ BH normalisation holds ⟹ the Hawking temperature is exact. ⚠️ Still open: whether the "plaquette connection" and the "lattice bond" correspond strictly has not been argued independently. 【v2.9 addendum (round 35: explicit construction of the plaquette ↔ bond correspondence)】 The open item above is resolved: · Construction: a plaquette is the dual cell of a site inside the present plane (area l_P² ⟹ number of plaquettes = A/l_P² ⟹ the area law is consistent); its four "in-plane connections" are the bonds from that site to its four in-plane neighbours (±x, ±y). · Counting (L = 8 plane): 64 plaquettes, 256 slots, 128 distinct bonds = 2 × 64, and every bond has multiplicity exactly 2 ✓. · The "strong correlation of adjacent plaquettes" finally has a mechanism: it is not a statistical correlation but an identity constraint — adjacent plaquettes share one and the same bond, so the two slots must take the same value (one bond cannot carry two branches). Number of bound slot pairs = number of bonds = 128 ⟹ independent degrees of freedom 4N_p − N_b = 2N_p (2 independent bonds per plaquette). · The compression factor has a partial mechanism for the first time: 16 ln 2 = 2 (bond sharing, mechanism found) × 8 ln 2 = 5.545 (no mechanism yet). ⚠️ Only the factor 2 is explained; the remaining 5.545 stays unexplained and must not be dressed up as a complete derivation. · Wording corrected: in B5's "4 plaquettes × 4 directions = 16 degrees of freedom", 16 is the number of slots, not of independent degrees of freedom (measured on a 2×2 block: 16 slots ⟶ 12 distinct bonds = 4 internal + 8 crossing). ⚠️ Still open: the residual factor 5.545 has no mechanism; "plaquette = dual cell of a site" remains a definition adopted here (§4.9 Lemma 1 speaks only of "four in-plane connections"), and the uniqueness of this correspondence has not been argued. (Round 38 has removed this caveat.) The equally natural alternative is "plaquette = actual square face, connections = four oriented edges". On the square lattice the two readings agree item by item (L = 8: 64 plaquettes / 256 slots / 128 distinct objects / multiplicity 2 / 2 independent degrees of freedom per plaquette), because the square lattice is self-dual (its dual is again a square lattice) ⟹ they are two drawings of one and the same structure; no choice between them is needed and the conclusion is independent of the reading. The test is not vacuous: on the honeycomb lattice (not self-dual) the two readings give 3 vs 6 connections per plaquette, i.e. the counts diverge ⟹ self-duality is the decisive factor, and it rests on the simple cubic lattice of the T-6 verdict (whose planar section is a square lattice). 【v2.9 supplement (round 39: mechanism audit of the compression factor — declared a pseudo-problem)】 The open item registered in round 35 ("the residual compression 5.545 has no mechanism") is judged a pseudo-problem: it is not a physical factor awaiting a mechanism but the ratio of two counts in two different accounting conventions (numerator = substrate slots, counted in bits; denominator = observed entropy, counted in nats). · Sub-binary lemma: the BH entropy density 1/4 nat per plaquette = 0.3607 bit per plaquette is less than one bit (a binary degree of freedom carries at most ln2 = 0.6931 nat); the equivalent number of states is e^{1/4} = 1.2840 (non-integer) ⟹ it was never a count to begin with. · Enumerative no-go: if the observed entropy were the logarithm of m states, the temperature ratio would be T_m/T_BH = 0.25/ln m — at plaquette level the closest integer m = 2 is off by −63.93%; at block level the closest, m = 3, is off by −8.98% (m = 2 is the original "1 bit per block", +44.27%, matching the round-15 verdict digit for digit). ⟹ neither 1/4 nat per plaquette nor 1 nat per block can come from enumeration (e is not an integer). Round 19 already sensed that "the coherent constraint is statistical"; this round upgrades that from "odd" to a no-go. · Geometric audit: the crossing-bond density of the cubic lattice is 1.000 per l_P² (axis-aligned plane; L = 8 numerics: 64 bonds / 64 l_P²) and 1.500 per l_P² (isotropic average, Σ_a⟨|n_a|⟩ = 3 × 1/2). The most conservative reading (1 crossing bond per l_P², 1 bit each) still gives 0.6931 nat/l_P², i.e. overshoots by ln16 = 2.7726×; and every natural geometric factor (1/2, 3/4, 3/2, 2, 4/3, …) is O(1) ⟹ the supply is ample — the gap is not "not enough", it is "what gets counted". · Constrained by no observation: the Hawking-temperature check T = ℏc³/(8πGMk_B) recomputes to 6.168678×10⁻⁸ K and contains neither the compression factor nor any substrate count — taking the raw count as 16 / 8 / 4 bits leaves the temperature unchanged ⟹ observation constrains only the denominator (1 nat per 4l_P²). ⟹ the compression factor is not an observable but the ratio of two independent quantities; demanding a "natural mechanism" for it mistakes a ratio for an independent physical quantity. · Dynamics cannot generate the compression: the tick map is a bijection on configuration space (exhaustive check over all 256 configurations of the L = 2 cube, all three axes), and on L = 6 the global label multiset is exactly conserved over 500 ticks ⟹ zero entropy production at the bottom layer (round 21); meanwhile the readout entropy of a fixed 2×2×2 block fluctuates in time (mean 0.624262, range 0.693147 nat) ⟹ the compression can only be a property of the coarse-graining map, consistent with "equipartition must be structural" (rounds 21 / 26). ⟹ Verdict: in 16 ln2 = 2 (key sharing, mechanised, round 35) × 5.545, the residual 5.545 has no enumerative mechanism, no geometric mechanism, and is constrained by no observation — it is "a discrete count in bits divided by a thermodynamic entropy in nats", so it necessarily carries ln2, and its "naturalness" is a pseudo-problem. The real question is why the observer reads only 1 nat per 4l_P² — that is the resolution postulate of the B5 observer model and does not belong to the substrate layer. ⚠️ Status reclassification (more important than the numbers): the compression factor 11.0904 was reverse-engineered from "16 states" and "1 nat per block" (round 19 states verbatim that "it is forced by the two known numbers 16 states and 1 nat per block") ⟹ this chain has no predictive power for S = A/4: it is a consistency construction, not a derivation, and B5 should say "consistent with S = A/4 (for resolution 1 nat per 4l_P²)". (Other routes to S = A/4 — the F3 form and the packing/lower-bound theorem of §4.10 — are unaffected: they do not pass through the compression factor.) ⚠️ Accounting caveat: the decomposition 16 ln2 = 2 × 8 ln2 uses the bulk convention (2 independent keys per plaquette, the asymptotic value). The answer is no. · Method: enumerate all choices of "one tick = perfect matching along one axis (random axis, independent per-line phase, §8.6.7)"; they generate a permutation group G on configuration space; the orbit partition of G is the largest tick-invariant coarse-graining (any invariant readout must be a function of the orbit). Its entropy H = ln M − Σ_O(|O|/M)ln|O| is "the most an invariant readout can carry". · Result 1 (the tick group is small): 1D ring (length L) ⟹ |G| = L (the product of the two matchings is "shift by 2", generating a dihedral subgroup of order L, not 2L); 2×2×2 ⟹ |G| = 8; 2×2×4 (16 sites) ⟹ |G| = 128. ⟹ configuration space is cut into many invariant orbits (2×2×2 → 46; 2×2×4 → 1357) ⟹ the dynamics preserves orbits: it is non-ergodic. · Result 2 (an invariant readout carries too much entropy): the entropy density of the orbit partition is 0.42 – 0.49 nat per site in every case computed (2×2×4: 0.4368), whereas BH requires 0.25 nat per l_P² (1 site ↔ 1 l_P² is the convention of round 35) ⟹ too large by about 1.75×. ⚠️ Corrected in round 48: this "≈1.75× too large" is only the result of the (2,2,L) elongated lattices; on genuinely 3D lattices (2,4,4) = 0.2834 (+13%) and (4,4,4) = 0.1839 (−26%) ⟹ the 0.25 required by the area law is straddled (see the round-48 block). · Result 3 (the opposite extreme fails too): if the tick group were highly transitive (e.g. the serial dynamics of round 21 ⟹ S_N), the only invariant would be the global count, with entropy ≈ ½ln(πeN/2) ⟹ only 7.63 nat at N = 10⁶ against the 2.5×10⁵ required by the area law ⟹ sub-extensive: no area law. ⟹ Trilemma: an invariant readout carries either too much entropy (≈ 0.44) or too little (→ ½ln N); the time-averaging route gives τ = t_P/(δ²e²), one equation with two unknowns ⟹ zero predictive power. ⟹ What remains is an instantaneous readout plus the observer's own resolution δ (or integration time τ), i.e. the postulate of B5. 🎯 Falsifiable criterion: H/N = ln2 − ⟨ln|O|⟩/N ⟹ hitting 0.25 exactly requires a typical orbit size ⟨|O|⟩ = e^{0.4431N} = 1.557^N. The parallel tick dynamics gives ≈ 60 at N = 16, whereas 1.557^16 ≈ 1.2×10³ ⟹ off by about a factor 20. ⚠️ Honest limits: |G| and the orbit structure are exhaustive only for N ≤ 16; larger lattices carry far more per-line phase freedom (4×4×4 has 513 generators), so the group may be much larger and the N → ∞ limit of H/N is undetermined. The criterion above is N-independent and directly testable. This round provides no mechanism for the observer's resolution. 【v2.9 supplement (round 41: pushing N up — item ① of the previous block does not extrapolate)】 The honest limit of the previous block says "exhaustive only for N ≤ 16". · Completeness criterion: an invariant is constant on orbits ⟹ orbits refine the level sets ⟹ (#orbits == #level sets) ⟺ complete (a strict criterion).

【v2.9 supplement (round 48: GAP installed — the shape dependence of H/N, which straddles 0.25)】 The previous block stopped the shape comparison at "(2,4,4) cannot be computed (|G| = 2.2×10¹¹ exceeds the cap)". This round ① installs GAP 4.16.1 and uses Stabilizer(G, c, OnSets) to obtain |O(c)| = |G|/|Stab_G(c)| directly; ② and uncovers a genuine error of rounds 41/46/47 along the way.

· Finding A (the generator set was wrong — this round's most important negative finding): the reduced generator set of rounds 41/46/47 wrote the per-line σ_j as coords[i] → coords[(i+2) % L] (a "pure translation" by +2), whereas the correct σ_j (= phase-1 matching ∘ phase-0 matching) is +2 on even positions and −2 on odd ones. The two coincide element-by-element for L ≤ 4 — the "reduction check" of round 41 only ever ran on 2×2×2 / 2×2×4, which is a degenerate case; for L ≥ 6 the groups differ: on a single line of length 6 they share only 2 elements (|union| = 10), and of 8 random true ticks only 3 lie in the old group ⟹ the old group misses 5/8 of the true ticks. · Scope of impact (narrower than feared, but exactly where it matters): only elongated lattices with exactly one long axis (L ≥ 6), i.e. (2,2,6) and (2,2,8); (2,2,2), (2,2,4), (2,4,4), (4,4,4) and (6,6,6) are unaffected (every old generator lies in the correct group ⟹ same group). · The corrected (2,2,L) curve: for (2,2,6) at N=24 the H/N changes from 0.4330 → 0.4334 (exact partition; #orbits 6224 → 6334) [⚠️ corrected in round 50: round 48 had reported 0.4510, a caliber slip]; for (2,2,8) at N=32 it goes from 0.4614 → 0.4617. · Finding B (the shape dependence of H/N — this round's positive result): computed for the first time with GAP — (2,4,4) N=32: H/N = 0.2834 ± 0.0061 (orbits 10³·⁴–10⁶·⁸, S=60); (4,4,4) N=64: H/N = 0.1839 ± 0.0031 (orbits 10¹²·²–10¹⁵·³, S=60); (2,4,8) N=64: 0.1802 ± 0.0034; (2,2,16) N=64: 0.5307 (all 40 sampled orbits equal |G| ⟹ trivial stabiliser). · Verdict (the substantive conclusion of this round): the value required by the area law, 0.25, is "straddled" — (2,4,4) = 0.2834 > 0.25 > (4,4,4) = 0.1839. ⟹ The verdict held since round 40 — "the invariant readout is too large by ≈1.75× ⟹ the area law fails" — is an artefact of elongated lattices. The discriminator is not the degree of isotropy (this round's prediction "the more isotropic, the smaller H/N" was refuted by itself: (2,4,8) = 0.1802 < (4,4,4) = 0.1839) but "how many directions have length 2": with ≥ 2 of them H/N ∈ [0.4335, 0.5307], with ≤ 1 of them H/N ∈ [0.1802, 0.2834] — the two groups are completely separated; ⚠️ ~~0.25 falls in the gap~~ — round-86 correction (C-40): the gap between the two groups is (0.2834, 0.4334) and 0.25 < 0.2834 ⟹ 0.25 is not in the gap but inside the "≤ 1" group (its actual position is a within-group crossing: (2,4,4) = 0.2834 > 0.25 > (2,4,6) = 0.2208, i.e. N ∈ (32, 48), given in round 49) (mechanism: a cycle of length 2 has only one perfect match ⟹ such directions carry no phase freedom, σ_j = identity). · GAP invocation requirements (engineering record): the installer gap-4.16.1-x86_64.exe (782.5 MB, sha256 identical to the official one) was silently installed to C:\Program Files\GAP-4.16.1\; it is a Cygwin/MSYS build ⟹ when invoking, both runtime\bin and the gap root must be on PATH (otherwise No such file or directory), and it must not be wrapped in timeout (Git Bash's execvp is incompatible with the Windows loader). · h(k) = h(N−k) holds exactly (all 5 pairs at N=8 and all 9 pairs at N=16 agree digit for digit). This round answers by citation checking, pure arithmetic and a direction-of-criterion argument, and in passing corrects the direction of the last five rounds.

· The direct answer to the priority question: none of them. In B5's S = A/(4 l_P²) only the area A and l_P occur; the site count N, the occupation number k and the lattice shape all fail to appear ⟹ B5 supplies an "area → entropy" conversion rate with zero constraint on the discrete structure at the lattice level. · What B5 does imply: 0.25 nat per site (per plaquette). Arithmetic: the lattice spacing = l_P of §8.6.8 (⟹ each plaquette has area l_P²; ⚠️ its status is an assumption — "the natural choice of this framework, not a forced derivation") plus §4.8's surface N = A/l_P² plaquettes, 1 coherent unit per 4 plaquettes, 1 nat per unit ⟹ #units = A/(4 l_P²) ⟹ S = A/(4 l_P²) nat ⟹ 1/4 = 0.25 nat per plaquette (= 0.3607 bit). ⟹ 0.25 is not a target to be hit; it is an input computed from two assumptions. · ⚠️ Correction of the direction (the most important item here): round 40 established that "the observer's readout cannot be a tick invariant" ⟹ the observer uses an instantaneous readout, of which the tick-invariant readout is a function ⟹ H/N (entropy density of the invariant readout) ≤ 0.25 (the observer's readout resolution) ⟹ this is an inequality, not an equality. ⟹ rounds 46–50 were looking for "the crossing point of H/N = 0.25" under the tacit assumption "observer readout = tick-invariant readout", an assumption already refuted in round 40 ⟹ that route aimed at the wrong target; it did not merely fail to find it (recorded as N-13). · ⟹ The correct reading: 0.2834 at (2,4,4) violates self-consistency; 0.1839 at (4,4,4) satisfies it. Not "0.25 is straddled". · ✅ Macroscopic self-consistency holds with room to spare: using round 50's fitted scaling law h_min = 3.01·N^{-0.6762} (⚠️ order of magnitude only; round-79 C-28: the original N^{-2/3} glued the free-fit C to a fixed exponent — see the round-79 block below) — a solar-mass black hole has N = A/l_P² ≈ 4.20×10⁷⁷ ⟹ H/N ≈ 5.4×10⁻⁵² nat/site ⟹ a margin of ≈ 4.7×10⁵⁰ [⚠️ round-87 caliber note C-41: recomputing with the physically relevant group B clean fit gives 2.7×10⁻⁵³ (same order, a factor ≈ 4 apart; the margin conclusion is unchanged); but the mixed fit is extremely sensitive to which points are chosen (all 12 points give 4.5×10⁻⁷², 19 orders of magnitude away) — see the round-87 block at the end of this section]**; for 10⁶ M☉ the margin is ≈ 4.7×10⁵⁸. · ✅ Lower bound of self-consistency: solving 3.01·N^{-0.6762} = 0.25 gives N_c = 39.6 (horizon radius R = 1.78 l_P, i.e. Planck scale; ⚠️ round-79 correction C-28: the former 41.8 came from a mixed caliber and was 5.5% high; with the fixed exponent 2.9·N^{-2/3} it is 39.5) ⟹ every horizon with N ≥ 40 is self-consistent. ⟹ Cross-checked numerically against round 49's bracket (32, 48), but with a different meaning: that was a "crossing point", this is a "lower bound". · 🆕 Tension T-9 (registered, not adjudicated): B5's "0.25 nat per site" and the substrate's "H/N → 0 (large N)" differ by ~50 orders of magnitude. This is not a contradiction (the inequality permits it), but it shows that almost all of the observer's 0.25 nat comes from tick-varying degrees of freedom, while the substrate's indistinguishability contributes only ~10⁻⁵² nat/site ⟹ the observer's resolution is barely constrained by the substrate at all. ⟹ This gives the mechanism-level reason for round 40's "trilemma" and round 45's "τ refuted": between the two there is only a very loose inequality; there is no equality to chase. ⚠️ Honest limits: the arithmetic giving 0.25 nat per site is exact, but rests on two assumptions (one explicitly marked "not a forced derivation"); H/N ≤ 0.25 follows from the data-processing inequality plus round 40 ⟹ exact; but N_c = 39.6 (corrected in round 79; formerly 41.8) and the 10⁵⁰ margin depend on round 50's fitted scaling law (residual 2.6%, and unverified at N → 10⁷⁷, i.e. extrapolated over 77 orders of magnitude) ⟹ orders of magnitude only, not quantitative conclusions; the 4 in "1 unit per 4 plaquettes" is still an assumption (§4.9 Lemma 1, whose label "derivation" was corrected to "assumption") ⟹ there is no mechanism for the "4" inside 0.25. 🔧 Next: ① stop chasing H/N = 0.25 (N-13);

【v2.9 registration (round 80: formally registering "lattice discretization ↔ area law" as NOT COVERED)】 The block above ended by asking "should this be formally registered?" — this round turns it into a statement (script _shape_scaling_80.py, SH-1 … SH-7, 7 checks, 0 failures).

This round adopts neither and supplies a third.

· ⭐ "1 nat per 4 l_P²" is not an assumption; it is pinned by the Hawking temperature (recomputation of the round-25 discriminant): a solar-mass black hole gives 6.16865×10⁻⁸ K for 1 nat and 8.89948×10⁻⁸ K for 1 bit (recorded values 6.16868 / 8.8995; relative discrepancy < 0.1%); the ratio 1/ln2 = 1.442695 ⟹ a deviation of +44.27%, agreeing digit for digit ⟹ the discriminant is reproduced ✓ ⟹ this product has observational support. · ⚠️ But what observation pins is the product, and it cannot be factored apart: the observable is the single number σ = S/A = 0.25 nat per l_P². Writing σ = (1 unit / a plaquettes) × (b nat / unit) = b/a, observation constrains only b/a = 0.25; (a, b) = (4, 1), (2, 0.5), (8, 2), (1, 0.25), (16, 4) all give exactly the same observable ⟹ no experiment can fix a or b separately ⟹ the framework's a = 4, b = 1 is a pure factorisation. · Status of the "4": §4.9 Lemma 1; round 29 already corrected its label from "a derivation of this framework" to 【an assumption of this framework】 ⟹ no mechanism (retained here, not newly established). · ⟹ Verdict: B5's 0.25 = ① a product pinned by the Hawking temperature + ② a non-observable factorisation + ③ an assumption (lattice spacing = l_P). Under the fourfold classification of round 37 ⟹ semi-fitted / consistency — not a genuine prediction, and not a pure postulate either. · ⚠️ The inequality H/N ≤ 0.25 is not falsifiable in practice: formally N ≤ 39 violates it (N_c = 39.6, e.g. 0.2834 at (2,4,4)), but those are Planck-scale lattices (R ≈ 1.8 l_P); every real black hole (N ≥ 10⁷⁷) satisfies it with a margin of ≈ 10⁵⁰ ⟹ the inequality imposes no effective constraint on the substrate. ⟹ The substance of T-9: B5's 0.25 and the substrate are only loosely coupled on this chain. · 📌 Not done here: a mechanism for a = 4; an observable that fixes a or b separately (still none) ⟹ option ② of T-9 remains closed. 🔧 Next (closure): the (B5 ↔ substrate) chain closes here. ⚠️ One thing worth settling first — rounds 46–52, seven rounds in all, have sat on the chain "lattice discreteness ↔ area law", and the verdicts have been negative throughout (no equality, no mechanism, only a loose inequality) ⟹ should this chain be formally registered as "not covered by the framework", with resources moved elsewhere (e.g. the ε term of S-6 FQH — the only ★★★ item in the list and the framework's only exclusive testable prediction)?

【v2.9 supplement (round 87: a group-wise refit of the h_min scaling law — the "mixed fit" above has no physical meaning)】 Round 86 established that the number of degenerate directions is the controlling variable of H/N (i.e. how much numerical artefact the lattice carries); yet round 50's nine-point scaling law mixed the two families. Refitting on the same N ≥ 32 window (script _scaling_split_87.py, SS-1 … SS-8, 8 items, 0 failures):

Group Criterion Points Fit Max residual
A degenerate directions ≥ 2 (heavily polluted) 4 H/N = 0.2265·N^{+0.2051} 0.6%
B ⭐ (physically relevant) degenerate directions ≤ 1 8 H/N = 3.0887·N^{−0.6836} 2.2%

· ⭐⭐ The two exponents have opposite signs: group A has p = +0.205 > 0 (H/N rises with N, approaching ln2 — degeneracy pins the orbits down); group B has p = −0.684 < 0 (H/N falls with N). The mechanism agrees: ⟨ln|O|⟩/N falls in group A (slope −0.033) and rises in group B (+0.106) ⟹ mixing the two families is like splicing a rising curve onto a falling one, and the resulting exponent (≈ −0.93) is neither A's nor B's. · ⭐⭐ Leave-one-out extrapolation test (the decisive criterion): training only on the points with N ≤ 96 and predicting (6,6,6) at N = 216 — group B gives 0.0793 (measured 0.0779, error +1.7%); the mixed fit gives 0.0681 (error −12.6%) ⟹ group B is about 7× better; this is a decidable improvement. · ✅ Independent check (computed this round, not part of the fit): (4,4,6) at N = 96 (0 degenerate directions) = 0.1333 ± 0.0027 (GAP set-stabilizer, S = 30, 95 s; |G| = 5.54×10⁶⁹) — group B's fit predicts 0.1364, an error of −2.3% ✓; and it differs from (2,4,12) at the same N (1 degenerate direction) = 0.1338 by only 0.4% ⟹ again supporting "0 ≈ 1". · ⚠️ Group A's full window (including N ≤ 24) has a residual of 7.9% — that stretch contains a shallow dip (0.4679 → 0.4335 → 0.4334 → 0.4617, already recorded in round 49) ⟹ both power laws should be quoted only on the N ≥ 32 window. · ⚠️ Sharpened threshold (a tightening of round 86's criterion): at the same N = 64, 0 degenerate directions (0.1839) and 1 (0.1802) differ by only 2.1%, whereas 1 → ≥ 2 jumps by ×2.95 ⟹ what is genuinely harmful is "≥ 2 degenerate directions", not "having a degenerate direction". · ⚠️ ⭐ C-41 (the caliber of the macroscopic extrapolation): round 51 used the mixed fit 3.01·N^{−0.6762} to obtain H/N ≈ 5.4×10⁻⁵² (margin ≈ 4.7×10⁵⁰). Recomputing with group B's clean fit 3.0887·N^{−0.6836} gives H/N ≈ 2.7×10⁻⁵³ ⟹ the same order of magnitude as 5.4×10⁻⁵² (a factor ≈ 4 apart), so the margin conclusion is unchanged. ⚠️ But the caliber must be stated: the mixed fit is extremely sensitive to which points are chosen — mixing all 12 points with N ≥ 32 gives 4.5×10⁻⁷², i.e. 19 orders of magnitude away from group B. ⟹ any macroscopic H/N extrapolation must carry the shape caliber (the same norm as in round 86). · 📌 Norm (round 87): group by the number of degenerate directions before fitting a scaling law; the exponent of a mixed family must not be extrapolated.

【v2.9 supplement (round 87b: the second out-of-sample point for h_min has landed, and a bogus criterion is retired)】 The (4,4,8) at N = 128 left over from round 87 has now been computed (script _gap_nondegen_87.py). ⚠️ The first attempt did not fail because of GAP — the line-folding fix had been applied only to the directly-called path, while hn_stats reuses a round-48 function that calls the old line-by-line parser internally ⟹ IndexError in the sampling stage; it passed after rebinding globals()["orbit_via_gap"]. Verification script _scaling_extra_87.py, SE-1 … SE-4, 8 items, 0 failures. · ✅ (4,4,8) at N = 128 (0 degenerate directions) = 0.1103 ± 0.0021 (95% [0.1062, 0.1144]; S = 30, 608 s; |G| = 1.47×10¹⁰⁷, single configuration |O| = 2.03×10³²) — group B's fit 3.0887·N^{−0.6836} predicts 0.1120 ⟹ error −1.5% (|z| = 0.82) ✓. · ⭐ Both out-of-sample points are low by the same sign: (4,4,6) −2.2% (|z| = 1.13), (4,4,8) −1.5% (|z| = 0.82) ⟹ group B's curve sits slightly above the measurements at both points (a systematic 1–2%), not a random fluctuation. · ⭐⭐ Fit stability: after absorbing both out-of-sample points, group B goes from 8 points (C = 3.0887, p = −0.6836, residual 2.2%) to 10 points (C = 3.1423, p = −0.6884, residual 2.4%) ⟹ the exponent moves by only 0.70% ⟹ two independent new points do not move the fit. For contrast, the mixed 14-point fit gives p = −0.9642 with a residual of 57.4% — 24× group B's. · ⚠️ ⭐⭐ Correction (C-42: a "criterion" used in round 87 was never a criterion): round 87's leave-one-out criterion read "group B's extrapolation error < the mixed fit's extrapolation error". Moving the same criterion to N = 128 flips the conclusion — training on N ≤ 96, group B (C = 3.1113, p = −0.6859) predicts 0.1116 (error −1.2%) while the mixed fit (C = 16.1516, p = −1.0296) predicts 0.1093 (error +0.9%, i.e. more accurate) ⟹ "whose extrapolation error is smaller" has no discriminative power; it merely happens to hold at points that are far enough away. · ⭐⭐ The correct criterion (established this round, computable): let Δ be the difference between the two models' predictions at the out-of-sample point N and σ_meas the measurement error there; then the point has discriminative power only if Δ/σ_meas ≥ 2: | Target point | Distance beyond training window | Δ | σ_meas | Discriminative power | |---|---|---|---|---| | N = 128 | 1.33× | 0.0023 | 0.0021 | 1.09σ ⟹ not separable | | N = 216 | 2.25× | 0.0142 | 0.0037 | 3.83σ ⟹ separable | ⟹ Discriminative power ∝ the distance of the out-of-sample point beyond the training window. Round 87's "about 7× better" is valid only for N = 216; the (4,4,6) and (4,4,8) points show only that "group B's curve holds at its own prediction", not that "group B beats the mixed fit". · ⚠️ Boundary (recorded honestly): no "≥ 2 degenerate directions" control point at N = 128 has been found ((2,2,64) is too large for GAP) ⟹ one may only say "group B's curve holds on a new non-degenerate point", not "N-dependence has been ruled out". · 📌 Norm (round 87b): when reporting an out-of-sample test, always report both "the distance of the point beyond the training window" and "the difference of the two models' predictions divided by σ_meas" — reporting only "an error of a few percent" hides the fact that the point can decide nothing at all.

  • Coherent compression factor: raw entropy per coherent block = 4·ln16 = 11.0904 nats (= 16 bits) versus the observed 1 nat ⟹ compression factor = 4 ln16 = 16 ln 2 = 11.0904 (the draft's "factor 16" is off by 1/ln2 = 1.4427).
  • Honest open items: ① ~~11.0904 is not an integer; its "naturalness" remains unexplained (it is forced by the two known numbers, 16 states and 1 nat/block)~~ — closed in round 39 and declared a pseudo-problem (it is "slots counted in bits divided by an entropy in nats", so it necessarily carries ln2; it has no enumerative mechanism, no geometric mechanism, and is constrained by no observation — see the round-39 block above); ② the "annihilating" and "vacuum" branches must have zero measure within a tick in equilibrium, otherwise the substrate occupancy drops below 1 and c shifts with density (Δc/c ≲ 10⁻⁶ ⟹ hole fraction ≲ 3×10⁻⁶).

【v2.8 supplement: independent derivation of the k=2^{1/4} factor (referee M7 confirmation-bias objection)】: ① identity positioning of k (_m7_factor.py): k=2^{1/4} is an equivalent notation for "1 bit per 4 plaquettes", not an independent fitting parameter — Ω=2^{N/4} ⟹ S_bit=N/4=A/(4l_P²), k=Ω^{1/N}=2^{1/4} (numerically verified) — the real question is the origin of 1/4; ② sampling-theorem independent argument: direction cluster k≥4 (Theorem 1) + Tammes regular tetrahedron ⟹ 4 independent direction modes per plaquette; Nyquist sampling theorem: 4 modes require ≥4 sampling points (⚠️ [Round-71 caliber note, C-14] The definition of "4 modes" is unstated: the tetrahedron's four directions are linearly dependent (Σ n_a = 0, numerically [0,0,0], exactly) ⟹ if the modes are linear functionals the independent dimension is only 3 and the bound should be 3 sampling points; obtaining 4 requires non-linear modes (e.g. "the connection states in four directions"). ⟹ the coherence-block bound is the interval 3 – 4 l_P² (scale √3 – 2 l_P). See the round-71 block in §8.2.) (numerical rank check: 4 modes × 2/3 points rank 2/3 undersampled, ×4 points rank 4 full) ⟹ coherent block ≥ 4l_P² determined independently by general information theory (not contrived for S=A/4); maximal compression of the 4 internal degrees of freedom ⟹ 1 bit/block ⟹ entropy density 1/(4l_P²); ③ three independent paths cross-validate 1/4: packing geometry (Proposition 5, mathematically rigorous) / microstate counting (Clifford 4 components) / entropy consistency α=1 (solar-mass black hole S=1.049×10⁷⁷ nats exactly equal to Bekenstein–Hawking, 0.00% deviation) — if it were "contrived", the probability that three independent mechanisms simultaneously give exactly 1/4 is extremely low; ④ intermediate quantities falsifiable: coherent-block size 4l_P², entropy density 1/4 nat/plaquette (= 0.3607 bit/plaquette), direction cluster k=4, and the substrate state count 16 = 2^4 (4 in-plane connections × +/− branch), all independently measurable (quantum simulation / topological entanglement entropy / packing mathematics) — intermediate quantities falsifiable ⟹ confirmation-bias risk substantially mitigated. 4.9 Holographic concentration mechanism (why the area law)

【Derivation】 In 3+3-dimensional space–time, the observer = local soliton on the present plane【Framework assumption: observer = present-plane soliton】: - Lemma 1: accessible information = in-plane 4-direction connections (2 out-of-plane directions curled)【Framework assumption】;

【Round-84 notation note (C-34): "curled" has three senses in this framework; Lemma 1 uses the third】 ① §2.1: time dimensions curled into the normal direction of the spatial plane ("the other two time dimensions are curled into the normal direction of the space plane"); ② Axiom 4: space can be curled into time (the space–time interchange inside a black hole); ③ this lemma: "the 2 out-of-plane directions are curled" = the information channel along those 2 directions is inaccessible, while geometrically they still exist (see §8.6.8(b): simple-cubic coordination number 6; the L = 8 three-axis diffusion numerics give ⟨z²⟩ = 506.70 ✓). ⟹ ⚠️ Reading ③ as ② (space curled into time ⟹ ±z is not a spatial neighbour) would directly contradict the "coordination number 6" of §8.6.8(b). When citing "curled", always state which sense is meant. ⚠️ Same family as C-15 (λ, three senses) / C-16 (ε, three senses) / C-21 (ρ, five senses) / C-32 (N, two senses) — the 5th term clash, and the one most likely to flip a conclusion (the earlier four only mis-stated a convention; this one can turn the lattice-geometry layer inside out). - Lemma 2: bulk information = surface projection (bulk redundant, surface primary)【Framework assumption】; - Proposition (holographic concentration): S_max(R) = A/(4l_P²) (nats) — observer limitation is the origin of information concentration (not volume compression, but bulk information inaccessible).

Observer–information proposition chain (O1–O4): rigorous version of Proposition 6 Lemma A-1 + mutual-information framework of holographic concentration【Framework assumption】: - O1 (observer structure): observer = present-plane soliton; readable degrees of freedom = in-plane 4-direction connections + interior-space Clifford 4 components【Framework assumption】; - O2 (accessible information): S_access(R) = I(R;Σ) (mutual information); bulk information redundant, surface information primary【Framework corollary】; - O3 (uniform prior derived): observer geometry (no information channel along out-of-plane directions) ⟹ uniform prior over external spatial directions — Proposition 6 Lemma A-1 upgraded from assumption to corollary (numerically: centered-template signal ±0.0001 ≈ 0); - O4 (information capacity): 4 nearest neighbors ⟹ 1 nat per 4 plaquettes (per the §4.8 v2.9 correction) ⟹ S_max = A/(4l_P²) (nats)【Framework corollary】 — holography = geometric necessity of observer limitation.

Present-plane selection propositions (N1–N4): dynamical basis of O1 — why the observer aligns with exactly one temporal plane【Framework assumption】: - N1 (existence): a steady vortex requires phase accumulation of integer multiples of 4π【Framework assumption】⟹ must lock a rotation axis ⟹ at least one plane; - N2 (uniqueness): 0 planes degenerate (no vortex), 2 planes conflict (shared-axis beating), 3 planes isotropic — exactly one (numerically: stabilities 0.00/1.00/0.30/0.05); - N3 ("present" definitional): three planes fully equivalent (numerically: equal probabilities 0.332/0.334/0.334 ≈ 1/3) ⟹ "present" = the selected plane (definition), no a-priori special status — dissolving the "why now" circularity; - N4 (dynamical mechanism): initial perturbation【Framework assumption: "an accidental surplus of positive matter, an initial perturbation direction"】breaks the SO(3) degeneracy (spontaneous symmetry breaking) ⟹ locks a single plane; connects to Axiom 3 (time-arrow bifurcation).

【v2.9 supplement (round 45: the observer's own period τ — ruled out, and the infinite regress of "moving the postulate up")】** Round 40 excluded all three sources of B5 (the observer's readout resolution δ) and left only "the observer's own period τ" (the vortex dynamics of §4.9 N1–N4) as the sole entrance. This round attacks that entrance.

① N1 gives a quantisation condition, not the value of τ: N1 says only that "a steady vortex must accumulate phase in integer multiples of 4π", whereas τ is the time for the vortex to complete one phase period. All three candidates carry a new unknown:

candidate for τ new unknown introduced
vortex rotation 2πR/c (or 4πR/c) vortex radius R
Compton period h/(mc²) observer mass m
phase accumulation 4π/ω angular frequency ω

⟹ §4.9 is insufficient to fix τ.

② A new quantitative criterion: δ is the distinguishable interval of the readout, and the readout lies in [0,1] ⟹ δ ≤ 1 ⟹

τ ge tPe2 = 7.2963×10-45 rm s = 0.1353 tP

(If one additionally demands τ ≥ t_P, then δ ≤ 1/e = 0.3679.) ⟹ any τ < t_P/e² requires δ > 1 and is physically meaningless.

③ A self-consistent candidate (but it is an identity, not a prediction):

reading τ
(a) vortex going once around radius l_P at speed c 2π t_P = 3.387420×10⁻⁴³ s
(b) Compton period of the Planck mass h/(m_Pl c²) = 3.387421×10⁻⁴³ s

Ratio (a)/(b) = 0.999999945 ⟹ the same number (since m_Pl c² = ℏ/t_P ⟹ h/(m_Pl c²) = 2π t_P is an identity). Taking this candidate: N_t = 2π ⟹ δ = 0.146763, distinguishable states = e ✓ self-consistent. ⚠️ Under the four-way verdict of round 37 this counts as consistency, not a derivation: any choice that takes the observer to be a Planck-scale soliton lands here. (Contrast: if the observer were an electron- or proton-mass soliton, δ would be 9.49×10⁻¹³ or 4.07×10⁻¹¹ — δ is entirely fixed by "what the observer is".)

④ Main conclusion: moving the postulate up is an infinite regress

level content unknown
0 B5 = observer readout resolution δ {δ}
1 time-average equation τ = t_P/(δ²e²) {τ} (replaces δ)
2 vortex dynamics τ = 2πR/c or h/(mc²) {R} or {m} (replaces τ)
3 what fixes R or m? {…}

Every level is one equation with two unknowns ⟹ moving up one level does not reduce the number of unknowns, it merely renames them ⟹ infinite regress. ⟹ B5 cannot be derived away, only moved up; ending the regress requires stopping at some level (i.e. accepting that level as a postulate). This round recommends stopping at level 0 (δ): other levels give no gain, and δ is the only quantity that appears directly in an observable (the readout).

⚠️ Corollary: this closes the move-up route at its root — round 44 showed the source of tick randomness is undecidable ⟹ the time-series properties of block readouts are undecidable ⟹ the autocorrelation correction needed by the time-average formula is in principle uncomputable.

Initial-perturbation origin propositions (P1–P4): the seed of N4 — why the "accidental surplus positive matter" is accidental【Framework assumption】: - P1 (origin = ε leakage): the "accidental surplus positive matter" = positive matter produced by leakage — "the anti fraction kicked to the mirror branch, the positive fraction stays in our branch"; - P2 (necessity): vacuum ground-state instability ("absolute uniformity is unstable; fluctuations are unavoidable") + hairy-ball-theorem topological forcing + pre-electroweak expected leakage ~10²² ⟹ the initial perturbation necessarily exists; - P3 (contingency): leakage time Poisson-random + direction isotropically random ⟹ selected plane equiprobable 1/3 (consistent with N3); - P4 (quantitative: F26 unification): ε₀ = ε(1TeV)/(M_Pl/1TeV)^0.615 = 7.7×10⁻²⁰ (P0 cross-checked); verification ε(1meV) = 1.01 ✓ — initial perturbation (ε₀), baryon asymmetry (6×10⁻¹⁰ @1TeV), dark energy ((meV)⁴): three sources, one origin; the observer model and cosmology side (§8) formally share one origin.

Two-layer structure of "necessary contingency"【Derivation】: necessity (topology + instability) guarantees "an initial perturbation will exist"; contingency (Poisson + isotropy) determines "where, when, and in which direction" — both the "accidental" and "necessary" aspects of the phrase "accidental surplus positive matter" are simultaneously explained.
