4.10 Black-hole interior as a 3D Kakeya set: packing proposition package

The full chapter draft is in the standalone Chinese file (Paper_Sec4.10_packing_theorem_package.md); this section is the embedded concise version.

【Framework assumption】Physical setting: black-hole interior = storm-overload region = 3D Kakeya set (contains all inward directions but information measure μ_info(V)≈0; information lives only in horizon-surface saturated modes); quantum line = Kakeya segment (cross-branch ER channel)【Framework assumption】; horizon = time-arrow-flip surface【Framework assumption】.

Theorem 1 (direction-cluster-number lower bound k ≥ 4)【New proof, rigorous】: Let V⊂S² be the direction cluster of quantum-line endpoints. If the covering radius is strictly < 90° (Kakeya "covers all directions" requirement, no zero-information-residual direction), then k ≥ 4.

【v2.9 provenance note】 This theorem is a special case of the Lusternik–Schnirelmann theorem on S² (LS category = 3), and shares its origin with the Borsuk theorem (Borsuk number of R³ = 4: any bounded set splits into 4 subsets of smaller diameter, and 4 is optimal). The independent proof below is valuable as a constructive version in the framework's language; citing LST/Borsuk in the main text is recommended, to avoid a referee noting that the wheel was reinvented. The analytic covering radii are k = 3 ⟹ 90.0000° (worst point = plane normal) and k = 4 ⟹ arccos(1/3) = 70.5288° (worst point = Voronoi vertex); the earlier 90.05° / 70.291° came from incomplete random search.

Proof: Suppose 3 open hemispheres cover S² ⟹ 0 ∈ int(conv{v₁,v₂,v₃}) (separating-hyperplane theorem) ⟹ vᵢ coplanar encircling ⟹ take plane normal p: p·vᵢ = 0 ∀i, covering radius exactly ≥ 90°, contradiction. ∎ (Analytic values: k = 3 ⟹ covering radius = 90.0000°, worst point the plane normal; k = 4 ⟹ arccos(1/3) = 70.5288°, worst point a Voronoi vertex. The old numbers 90.05° / 70.291° came from incomplete random search and are corrected.)

Theorem 2 (k=4 configuration)【Theorem】: Tammes n=4 — the unique optimal configuration of 4 points = regular tetrahedron, minimal angle arccos(−1/3) ≈ 109.47° (random 2000 trials optimal only 96.9°).

Theorem 3 (exact Voronoi partition)【Theorem, rigorous】: the regular tetrahedron's 4 directions partition S² into 4 congruent cells, each of area 4π/4 = π (Monte Carlo: 3.138–3.148 ≈ π).

Argument 4 ("exactly 4" = Clifford interior space)【Structural argument】: regular-tetrahedron 4 directions = interior-space component structure (Clifford Cl(1,3) representation dimension 4 = two time directions × two chirality directions); octahedron 6 directions = exterior-space axis structure (3 axes ±). Entropy encoding uses interior-space components ⟹ direction-cluster number 4. The three candidates (four quadrants / Hopf fibration / SU(2) double cover) unify in the same "4".

Proposition 5 (1/4 coefficient)【Derivation】: Theorem 1 (k≥4) → Theorem 2 (regular tetrahedron) → Theorem 3 (Voronoi π/cell) → Argument 4 (4=Clifford) → Nyquist (coherent cross-section 4l_P²) → 1 nat per unit (round-39 unit correction: it read "1 bit", with the conversion left dangling in the factor ln2 on the right) ⟹ S/k_B = A/(4l_P²) (nats), cross-validated with the §4.8 microstate counting as two paths.

Proposition 6 (interior-space encoding priority)【Derivation, framework-internal deduction】: the final step of Argument 4 — why entropy encoding uses the interior-space 4 components rather than the exterior-space 3 axes (6-direction octahedron). Three-lemma chain: - Lemma A (exterior-space entropy = 0): isotropy (Axiom 1) ⟹ no information in directional preference (uniform prior, numerically ‖⟨n⟩‖≈0); no-hair【Framework assumption: no-hair】⟹ exterior structure erased; event point-like-ness【Assumption H-8: event point-like-ness】 "two vortices dragged in different directions cannot coexist in the same micro-variation event" ⟹ directions are not intra-event attributes; - Lemma B (interior-space non-zero): quantum-line endpoints carry Clifford 4-component states latched across branches【Framework assumption】; Hawking radiation = leakage of interior-space states ⟹ the interior space is the information carrier; ⚠️ 【v2.9 supplement (round 55: the cross-branch channel carries information, not energy)】 Lemma B fixes the purpose of the cross-branch channel as information (Clifford 4-component state latching). This round adds an explicit boundary so that it cannot later be misused as an energy / matter conduit: · The framework's own energy condition does not support the negative energy density that "traversability" requires — §4.6 v1.24 states P(ρ) = P_P·tanh(ρ/ρ_P) ⟹ P ≤ ρc² everywhere, ∂P/∂ρ ≥ 0, and ρ ≥ 0. The framework has negative pressure (P < 0; gravity is exactly the "flux negative-pressure difference"), but no negative energy density — these are not the same quantity. · A traversable wormhole (Morris–Thorne 1988) requires violating the ANEC (throat ρ + P < 0) ⟹ in conflict with the energy condition above. Hence the literature comparison "YEM = traversable wormhole" in §4.4 must not be read as "this framework supports traversability", unless a negative-energy mechanism satisfying the quantum inequalities is first supplied. · Even granting ANEC violation, the quantum inequality caps the budget: ΔE·Δt ≲ ℏ ⟹ for Δt = 1 s, ΔE ≤ 1.06×10⁻³⁴ J, which is 14 orders of magnitude below room-temperature thermal energy (k_BT = 4.1×10⁻²¹ J) ⟹ it cannot drive any macroscopic work. ⟹ The cross-branch channel cannot serve as an energy / matter conduit within this framework. - Lemma C (4 = minimal complete spinor): Cl(1,3) spinor dimension 4 = two time directions × two chirality directions (1D trivial, 2D Weyl missing half, 4D Dirac complete — representation theory); - Proposition 6: entropy encoding = interior-space 4 components ⟹ direction-cluster number = 4. The three premises A1/A2/A3 are all existing framework assumptions; no new assumptions introduced.

Testable predictions (corollaries): B1 extreme-Kerr Hawking-radiation angular distribution deviates from axisymmetry (~10⁻⁸); B2 primordial black-hole entropy volume correction ΔS/S ~ l_P/R (~10⁻⁸); B3 Hawking-radiation–infalling-matter entanglement (information-flip verdict signature); B4 ER-bridge packing-density bound (BBH angular-mode distortion); B5 non-thermal correction ∝1/S_BH (~10⁻²⁰ window).

Honest notes: Theorems 1, 3 rigorous; Theorem 2 established; Argument 4 structural; Proposition 6 framework-internal deduction (three premises direct axioms); 【Assumption】 direction discretization (requires dynamical proof, §10.2.1).

【v2.9 supplement (round 56: can the framework define a quantity coaxial with "energy-storage capacity"? — Yes, but only in the f = O(1) tier)】 Round 55 audited the strategic goal "battery energy density" as not covered by the framework and left one decidable question: can the framework define a quantity coaxial with "energy-storage capacity"? This round answers it (script _storage_axis_56.py, SA-1…SA-12, 11 pass / 0 fail).

· Criterion: for Q ≡ ΔE/M to be coaxial it must satisfy ① two-factor structure (ΔE and M each computable) ② bound–dissociation difference (ΔE must be a difference of two states, not an absolute scale) ③ accounting-unit duality ④ releasability ⑤ reversibility. And it must be anchored on a dimensionless number: f ≡ Q/c² = ΔE_bind/(m_unit·c²) (the releasable mass fraction) — this is exactly what separates "storage" from "mass–energy equivalence": Li–O₂ has f = 1.385×10⁻¹⁰, gasoline 4.807×10⁻¹⁰, pure U-235 (complete fission) 9.135×10⁻⁴, mass–energy 1.000 (calibration check: Li–O₂ via 2Li + O₂ → Li₂O₂ gives 1.4798 eV/atom, matching the stoichiometric 5.92 eV / 4 digit for digit).

· Three quantities CAN be defined: ① Q_BH = T·S/M = c²/2 (black-hole thermodynamics): sympy symbolic elimination gives T·S = M c²/2 (M_Pl cancels after substituting l_P² = ℏG/c³); for a solar-mass BH, S/k_B = 1.049430×10⁷⁷, T = 6.168678×10⁻⁸ K, TS/(Mc²) = 0.500000000 ⟹ 4.4938×10¹⁶ J/kg = 1.2483×10¹³ Wh/kg. Dimensionally coaxial, zero conversion factors, not an identity — but it is a standard result (the reversible-process efficiency of black-hole thermodynamics), not framework-specific, and it gives only f = 0.5 (9.6 orders of magnitude from Li–O₂). ② Q_tube = c² (§5.7 "confinement = flux-tube linear potential": E = σd, tube mass M = σd/c² ⟹ σ, d, g, n_G all cancel) ⟹ an identity (round 53, criterion C-4: zero content), merely a restatement of "mass is energy". ③ Q_res = E(R)/m_unit (§4.6 F2 down-scaling mechanism E(R) = √(ℏc·E_P/R), δ = √(R·l_P)): R = l_P gives E_P (self-consistent ✓), R = 0.15 nm (real bond length) gives 4.0 PeV, R = R_H gives 4.19 meV, and 1 eV requires R = 78.06 kpc. ⟹ It is a coarse-graining cutoff scale, not a binding energy.

· ⭐ Structural obstruction (this round's main finding): the framework has four Hamiltonians but not one potential function — throughout the text, "potential energy / binding energy / interaction potential / dissociation / chemical bond / bond energy / internal energy / free energy" occur 0 times each, a radially-dependent V(r)/U(r) occurs 0 times, while "Hamiltonian" occurs 17 times. And all four Hamiltonians are "global energy rulers": H_event = Σp²/2m + ΣV_contact (V_contact with no functional form and no coupling constant), H_F = −JΣA_v − KΣB_p (J = n_G·E_P·g, Planck scale), H(λ) = −ΣA_cube − λΣX_face (same J; Δ = 2 and Δ±2 ≈ 0.49 are both in units of J), and the Lindblad layer (energy scale not given). ⟹ No second state ⟹ no ΔE_bind ⟹ no f ⟹ energy-storage capacity is undefined. 5 Gauge-Group Structure

5.1 "Pairing order" and the three gauge groups

【Derivation】 Define the pairing order n = the smallest positive integer such that the representation tensor product ⊗ⁿ contains the trivial representation:

Group n Representation theory Physics
U(1) 1 all one-dimensional, q⊗(−q)=1 long-range, massless photon
SU(2)_L 2 2 pseudo-real (2≅2̄, ε^ij sign-preserving) short-range (massive W/Z), chiral
SU(3)_c 3 3 complex (3≇3̄), ε_ijk third-order color confinement (qqq cube has no solution)

Representation-theoretic root of color confinement: colorless combinations of baryons qqq require the third-order antisymmetric invariant tensor ε_ijk — three quarks form a singlet via the third-order tensor. Mesons qq̄ use δ_i^j (n=2 structure).

【v2.5 definition revision (referee M6)】: the pairing order is precisely defined as n ≡ d_min(G) (smallest non-trivial irreducible representation dimension = number of color components on string-net edges), supplemented by the same-sign pairing order n′ (smallest n such that ρ⊗ⁿ without conjugation contains the trivial representation): U(1) n′=∞ (same-sign never contains trivial ⟹ must pair positive–anti = representation-theoretic root of charge conjugation C), SU(2) n′=2 (2⊗2=3⊕1, pseudo-real), SU(3) n′=3 (3⊗3⊗3=10⊕8⊕8⊕1, ε_ijk). Structural necessity: non-Abelian-ness ⟺ n′ finite ⟺ same-sign tensor products can form singlets (see §5.2 screening argument).

5.2 Screening argument (why SU(3)×SU(2)×U(1) — structural screening)

【v2.5 structural screening (referee M6)】: Given the pairing order n, the candidate group pool (U(1)/Z_N/SU(2)/Q₈/S₃/SO(3)/SU(3)/G₂/SU(4)/Sp(2), 10 total) is eliminated one by one by three criteria, with a unique survivor at each step (numerical verification in the M6 report, dimension conservation all ✓):

  • S1 dimension matching: d_min(G) = n (n = vortex winding-mode number, §5.3 input)
  • S2 same-sign pairing: n′(G) = n (non-Abelian-ness ⟺ n′ finite)
  • S3 third-order antisymmetry: n=3 requires ε_ijk (det=1 ⟹ unique to the SU(N) sequence)
n Candidates (d_min matching) Reason excluded Unique survivor
1 U(1), Z_N Z_N discrete, Villain limit = U(1) (v1.30 Theorem A generalized) U(1) (unique 1D connected compact Abelian Lie group)
2 SU(2), Q₈, Sp(2), S₃ Q₈ ⊂ SU(2) discrete; Sp(2) symplectic pseudo-real (chirality picks SU(2)); S₃ same class (d_min=2) SU(2) (2 pseudo-real → chiral)
3 SU(3), SO(3), G₂, SU(4), A₄/S₄/Δ(27) SO(3) n′=2 (3 real self-dual → 3⊗3 contains trivial → no confinement forcing); G₂ d_min=7; SU(4) n=4; A₄ etc. are SU(3) discrete subgroups SU(3) (3 complex 3≇3̄ + ε_ijk → qqq third-order singlet)

Decisive exclusion (SO(3)): although d_min(SO(3))=3, its 3-dimensional representation is real self-dual (3≅3̄) → same-sign pairing order n′=2 ≠ 3 (3⊗3=5⊕3⊕1 already contains trivial) → structurally incapable of third-order confinement — a direct answer to "why SU(3) rather than SO(3)".

n≥4 exclusion: three spatial dimensions (locked by the Kakeya dimension) ⟹ the fourth-order antisymmetric ε_ijkl requires 4-dimensional space; n=4 Fermat has no solution (original argument retained); SU(2)×U(1) cannot merge (chirality, original argument retained).

Discrete–continuum bridge (correcting the crude A2 mapping): the framework's numerical layer uses quantum-double D(G) discrete groups — Z_N⊂U(1), Q₈⊂SU(2) (i²=j²=k²=−I), S₃⊂SU(3) (even-permutation matrices, det=1) — with unique continuous parent groups; S₃ itself has d_min=2 and belongs to the n=2 class; it is used as an SU(3) permutation subgroup for non-Abelian numerical verification (v1.33).

Residual contingency localized: after screening uniqueness, the only remaining input = the micro-origin of the vortex winding-mode numbers n=1,2,3 (§5.3 assumption, not derivable from representation theory — honestly retained).

5.3 Three fermion generations = vortex winding modes

【Framework assumption】 Each fermion generation = excitation of the same vortex winding mode at different energy scales: first generation n=1, second n=2, third n=3. Fourth-generation prediction: n=4 vortices decay unstably at z>1000, extremely heavy and short-lived above 1 TeV.

5.4 Kaluza–Klein equivalence and terminology resolution

【Derivation】 Abandon the phrasing "fifth dimension = flux-field dimension"; instead: "the flux field Φ = low-energy projection of the KK scalar mode field (g₅₅), whose VEV determines coupling constants and masses". The mathematical identity of D[Φ]=S is the spectral action (S = Tr f(D²/Λ²), heat-kernel expansion a₁→R, a₂→F²).

5.5 Multi-representation unified field: four forces = partial-wave decomposition

【Derivation】 Decompose flux-field fluctuations δΦ(x) into Lorentz-group irreducibles:

Component Spin Fluctuation type Corresponding force
Φ scalar 0 isotropic Higgs/modulus field
A_μ vector 1 directional electromagnetism U(1)
h_μν tensor 2 second-order tensor gravity

New statement of unification: not "different limits of one operator" but "the fluctuation spectrum of one flux field" — the differentiation of forces = partial-wave decomposition of fluctuation modes. 【To be verified】 complete emergence of the non-Abelian components (SU(2)×SU(3)), mass gap, chiral dynamics.

Where a fifth force would sit (added in round 62): after the four forces, both structural extension slots are closed — (1) an extra gauge group at spin 1: excluded by §5.2's n >= 4 (in 3 dimensions ε_ijkl ≡ 0, since C(3,4) = 0); (2) spin >= 3: excluded by the Weinberg–Witten theorem (no massless j > 2 in flat spacetime), and the same Weinberg soft theorem classification bars spin 3 as well (spin 2 is the boundary).

⚠️ Key criterion: this table's "spin" column is equivalent to a "singular power of the soft limit" column — a "force" is a universal coupling enforced by the Weinberg soft theorem: spin 2 (soft limit ∝ 1/q ⟹ equivalence principle) and spin 1 (∝ 1/q ⟹ conserved charge) are enforced, whereas the spin-0 soft limit ∝ q⁰ is non-singular ⟹ it enforces no conserved charge ⟹ spin 0 is intrinsically not a "fundamental force" — this is the deeper reason the table labels Φ as "Higgs / modulus field".

⚠️ But the soft theorem does not forbid a non-universal long-range scalar coupling (which is exactly what a fifth force is in standard physics), and the framework happens to write one spin-0 component as Φ = the low-energy projection of a KK scalar modulus field (g₅₅) (§4.5b) — which in standard KK is precisely a radion / dilaton, i.e. a recognized fifth-force carrier. The framework says only one sentence about Φ ("its VEV determines the coupling constants and masses") and gives neither its mass nor its coupling ⟹ that slot's status = undefined (registered as I-17). If one assumes Φ inherits the string-net scalar-sector gap (Δ_scalar = 0.41 J), then m = 1.50×10²⁸ eV and the range is λ = 0.81 l_P ⟹ 29–55 orders of magnitude away from the fifth-force search windows (micron to galactic) — utterly unobservable.

5.6 Quantum-double algebra: the framework's gauge structure (v1.19)

【Derivation + Proposition】 The framework's three event-flow operations from Axiom 1 (encounter/annihilation/regeneration) + P2-A non-commutativization ⟹ Hopf-algebra structure, with the most natural discrete realization = quantum double D(G) = C[G]⋉F(G) (Drinfeld double). Proposition Content Nature

A2 Pairing order → group selection (v2.5 revision): discrete layer n=1→Z_N (⊂U(1)) / n=2→Q₈ (⊂SU(2), pseudo-real) / n=3→S₃ (⊂SU(3), minimal non-Abelian); continuous parent groups U(1)/SU(2)/SU(3) unique (§5.2 three-criterion screening, structural not fitting) 【Proposition】+ numerical

【Round-69 correction C-11: the caliber of the 2+1D gap】 This subsection originally wrote "gap Δ=1" without stating its normalization, and inconsistently with its own definition of J. Exact diagonalization (256 states) and the analytic form (E(a,b) = 2(a+b) − (N_v+N_p)) agree: E0 = −8.0000 (matching this subsection), E1 = −4.0000, Δ = E1 − E0 = 4J; and Δ = 4J holds for L = 2…50 (not a finite-size effect). Mechanism: flipping one edge X_e changes the sign of A_v at both endpoints ⟹ ΣA_v drops by 4; and Π_v A_v = 1 is an operator identity ⟹ defects must come in pairs ⟹ the minimal excitation is 4J (not 2J). ⭐ The real source of the discrepancy is "open vs periodic", not the dimension: periodic ⟹ Π A = 1 exists ⟹ pairs ⟹ 4J; open ⟹ no such identity ⟹ a single A_c can be flipped independently ⟹ 2J (§5.8's single/double cube is open-boundary: E0 = −1, Δ = 2.0000 ✓). ⚠️ Why the "1" cannot be reconstructed: at L = 2 three different normalizations (Δ/(2L), Δ/4, |E0|/(N_v+N_p)) all give exactly 1.000000 (because 2L = 4 = N_v + N_p) — a numerical degeneracy that cannot be resolved; only L ≥ 3 separates them (Δ/(2L) gives 0.667). ⟹ Lesson: a gap must always be reported together with the normalization of J and the boundary condition. Script _numfix_69.py (NC-1…NC-8, 8 pass / 0 fail).

【Derivation】 Minimal Lagrangian density (multi-representation version):

  • ⚠️【Round-72 notation note】g is the 4th pure input of Φ in this framework (alongside Λ of §4.5b and μ, λ of §6.4), with zero constraints (see §6.2's degeneracy check putting g = 0 as a special case); and g carries two meanings under one letter (§5.7's string-net geometric factor J = n_G·E_P·g) ⟹ see C-15.
  • Φ is the condensate representative of annihilation–regeneration noise (auxiliary field).

6.5 Geodesic equation

【Citation】 Variating the point-particle action S_pp = −m∫dτ gives the Γ equation; Newtonian limit a⃗ = −∇Φ_N. "The body is readjusted at each instant to minimize the pressure difference" ⟺ Φ_N = flux negative-pressure potential.

7 Foundations of Quantum Mechanics

7.1 The problem: classical event flow cannot yield Bell violation

【Citation + Derivation】 A classical hidden-variable flow field can only give CHSH ≤ 2, contradicting the experimental CHSH = 2√2 — the framework must be non-commutativized. The original work was already aware of this ("for this to survive, you must be able to derive 2√2 … a structural product of source events").

7.2 Non-commutative event flow

【Derivation】Proposition (non-commutative event flow ⇒ quantum correlations): upgrade the event-flow state from a classical probability distribution (commutative algebra L^∞) to an operator algebra (C* algebra), with "encounter–annihilation" events corresponding to non-commuting projection operators (Clifford-type {Π_μ, Π_ν}≠0). Then: (i) joint measurement of entangled pairs gives CHSH = 2√2 (Tsirelson bound); (ii) complementarity V² + I² ≤ 1 holds automatically; (iii) uncertainty ΔA·ΔB ≥ ½|⟨[A,B]⟩| holds automatically.

Non-commutative elements already in the framework: Clifford algebra (micro-variations do not commute), path integral (−1)^N_exchange (exchange sign), geometric origin of superposition states (global equivalence of the centers of positive/anti storms), quantum lines (events of common origin).

Numerical verification: Bell state |ψ⁻⟩ + optimal measurement basis ⟹ S = −2.8284 = −2√2 ✓; path-state inner product gives V²+I² = 1 ✓.

【Round-76 discriminating-power annotation】 The reproduction of S = −2√2 above uses only 2 qubits and Pauli matrices — tick / string net / A_v / B_p / flux / lattice appear nowhere ⟹ it is the Tsirelson theorem (an algebraic fact about operator algebras), not a framework-specific result; the proposition of this subsection has the form "if {Π_μ,Π_ν}≠0 then CHSH = 2√2", i.e. the premise already contains the conclusion. An item-by-item verdict on the framework's own non-commutative elements is given in the round-76 block at the end of §7.4.

7.3 Quantum walk: dynamics of the non-commutative event flow

【Derivation】 Discrete-time quantum walk U = S(C⊗I): coin flip C (Hadamard, non-commutative) + conditional shift S. Numerics: quantum σ ~ t (ballistic) vs classical √t (diffusive) — non-commutativization preserves coherence.

7.4 Positioning clarification

【Derivation】 Once non-commutativized, the framework's quantum part mathematically coincides with standard QM. The framework's unique contribution = providing an ontological motivation for "why quantum mechanics" (in the spirit of Gel'fand–Naimark: commutative C ⟺ classical probability; non-commutative C ⟺ quantum states). The positioning is 't Hooft cellular-automaton-like: an event flow that is deterministic given the tick sequence (each tick is a permutation ⟹ bijective, reversible, zero entropy production, §8.6.7), with a non-commutative observable algebra. ⚠️ This does not claim global determinism — the tick sequence itself is an exogenous input, and the provenance of its randomness is undecidable within the framework (see the adjudication record in the round-57 block below).

【v2.9 supplement (round 57: is the framework deterministic? — registering tension T-12, and quantifying "everything")】 This round is term counting + assembly of existing results + one small numerical run (script _determinism_57.py, DF-1…DF-12, 11 pass / 0 fail).

· Four readings of "everything" (quantitative): ① all possible histories are realized (many-worlds / branching) ❌ — a tick "selects one axis ＋ one phase per line" ⟹ single branch; ② all possible states lie in the state space ✅ (configuration space 2^N, or the C(N,k) layers); ③ every actual trajectory visits all possibilities ❌ — the tick group is non-ergodic (configuration space is cut into invariant orbits; a trajectory never leaves its own orbit); ④ within an orbit, statistically equiprobable ✅ (the uniform distribution is the unique invariant measure on each orbit, §8.6.3; verified numerically, term by term invariant ✓). Coverage in ③ (within one k layer, k = N/2): (2,2,2) 7.14% ⟹ (2,2,4) 0.395% ⟹ (2,2,6) 0.0158% ⟹ the larger N, the farther from "everything" (N rises only from 8 to 24, yet coverage drops by a factor of 450). Cross-check: this round also decomposed the full configuration space 2^N — 2×2×2 → 46 orbits, 2×2×4 → 1357 orbits, agreeing with round 40 term by term ✓.

· Correction to "the initial state determines everything": the framework's state is the substrate occupation pattern, which does not contain the tick sequence (the tick is an external input) ⟹ the precise version is "the initial state ＋ the entire tick sequence". ⟹ "The framework holds" does not imply "the universe is deterministic."

【v2.9 supplement (round 68: I-16 — formalizing the "position-operator gap", with a "position × energy × q-number" three-layer verdict)】 I-16 originally asked whether the gap can be formalized as an axiom-level proposition. ④ ⭐ Three-layer verdict table (this round's main conclusion):

⟹ No layer satisfies "has position + has energy + position is a q-number" simultaneously. ⟹ Precise form: the gap is not in "position" nor in "energy" but in "the quantization of position".

⑤ Combinatorialization of position (numerical): position enters the invariants of the substrate layer only through π(p) ∈ (Z₂)³ ⟹ the number of values of δ is bounded by 8, independent of N; the full pair-orbit counts are 7 / 9 / 10 (N = 8/16/24) — N grows 3× while the orbit count grows only 1.43×. Cross-validated between all tick permutations (3 / 18 after dedup) and the reduced generators with identical results ⟹ generator error is ruled out. ⚠️ Hence the correction C-10: line 425 (round-40 block) of this subsection states "pairs have exactly 8 orbits", which is inaccurate — measured 7 (N=8) / 9 (N=16) / 10 (N=24). Internal consistency alone decides it: for N=8 there are only C(8,2) = 28 unordered pairs, whereas 8 orbits × |O₂| = (N/4)² = 4 = 32 ≠ 28; 7 × 4 = 28 ✓. The same sentence's |O₂| = (N/4)² is correct (4 / 16 match digit for digit) ⟹ that sentence should read "δ takes at most 8 values; the orbit count is 7 / 9 / 10".

⑥ Energy does not know the Euclidean distance (quantitative): in the string tension E_pair(d) = 2E_m + σ_m·d, d is a number of lattice steps (on a cubic lattice = the L1 distance). (1,1,0) and (2,0,0) have the same L1 = 2 ⟹ the framework assigns the same energy, yet their Euclidean distance ratio is 2/√2 = 1.4142 (a 41.4% difference) ⟹ all directions with the same L1 are degenerate ⟹ no bond angle ⟹ no molecular geometry. A real V(R) depends on the Euclidean distance and has a minimum (a potential well) — whereas E_pair(d) is monotonically non-decreasing (σ_m = 0.21 / 1.09 / 3.04 at λ = 0.5 / 1.0 / 2.0; at λ = 0 it is flat, still no minimum) ⟹ no well ⟹ no bound state (structurally impossible, not "not yet computed").

⑦ Closing the loop: this supplies the structural reason for round 61's "sharpening" — "the framework can do a test particle in an external field" = the single-particle version of the pinning term (x is a given background, c-number); "it cannot do the arrangement of two comparable masses" = that requires position itself to be a q-number. The two are the front and back of a single structural property.

This is the only "necessity" argument in the framework backed by a rigorous external theorem. ⚠️ But what it proves is "if commutative then impossible" — not "the framework's observable algebra is commutative", which is §7.1's premise (unproved) and precisely what §7.2 sidesteps by the act of "upgrading to an operator algebra".

8 Cosmology

8.1 Energy-scale dependence of the leakage mechanism (ε_A)

Notation convention (important): the symbol ε carries two unrelated meanings in this paper, and they must be distinguished — ① leakage probability ε(E) (this chapter, §8.1 / §8.2, energy-scale dependent, defined by ε(E) = ε₀(M_Pl/E)^β): ε, ε₀, ε_A, ε_B, ε_topo, ε_eff in this section and §8.2 all carry this meaning; ⚠️ Round-73 annotation (C-16): ε carries a third meaning — the drift exponent of §8.4 (E_c(z) = E_c,0(1+z)^ε ⟹ w = −1 + 4ε/3), which this convention's scope ("this section and §8.2") does not cover. ⚠️ Forcing them to coincide and using the boundary condition below (ε(1 meV) ~ 1) would give w = +0.333, i.e. 43.9σ away from DESI+CMB (w = −1.028 ± 0.031) ⟹ they must differ; the formal declaration is in the round-73 block of §8.6.5. ② Levi-Civita totally antisymmetric tensor ε_ijk / ε^ij (the representation-theoretic notation of §5.2, energy-scale independent). ⟹ Whenever a value of ① is quoted, the energy scale must be stated (e.g. ε(1 TeV) = 6.0×10⁻¹⁰); cross-scale quotation is meaningless — this is precisely why round 53 failed S-E5 (N-14: ε₀ = ε(M_Pl) = 7.7×10⁻²⁰ was carried over to the FQH scale (0.1–15 meV), where it should be O(1) — a gap of 18–21 orders of magnitude). ⟹ This is the notation-hygiene rule registered in batch 0 (2026-09-28): from now on, every energy-scale-dependent quantity (ε, α, λ_c, …) must be followed by an (@E) annotation. (A parallel collision was already declared for λ in §8.6; see "Notation convention (important)" there.)

【Derivation】 ε_A(E) = ε₀(M_Pl/E)^β, two boundary conditions: - E ~ 1 TeV → ε ~ 6×10⁻¹⁰ (baryon asymmetry η ≈ ε); - E ~ 1 meV → ε ~ 1 (dark energy ρ_Λ ~ (meV)⁴); - β = log(6×10⁻¹⁰)/log(10⁻¹⁵) ≈ 0.615, ε₀ = 7.731×10⁻²⁰. (【round-53 caliber unification】 this section wrote 7.6×10⁻²⁰, the abstract and §6 wrote 7.7×10⁻²⁰, and §8.2 H3 wrote 7.67×10⁻²⁰ — a 2% spread; now unified to the value computed from the defining relation ε₀ = ε(1TeV)/(M_Pl/1TeV)^β. E_c is sensitive to ε₀, so this constant must be unique.)

Cutoff scale【Derivation】: probability normalization ε_A(E_c) = 1 ⟹ E_c = M_Pl·ε₀^{1/β} = 1.0181 meV — of the same order as the dark-energy characteristic scale 2.3 meV ((E_c)⁴ differs by a factor 27; O(1) coefficient undetermined).

【round-53 correction: E_c is an identity, not a【Derivation】】 substituting the definition of ε₀ gives E_c = M_Pl·[ε(1TeV)/(M_Pl/1TeV)^β]^{1/β} = 1TeV·ε(1TeV)^{1/β} — M_Pl cancels completely, value = 1.000000 meV exactly (the recorded 1.0181 meV is a numerical residual of −1.78% from the ε₀ / M_Pl values used). ⟹ since β ≡ ln(η)/ln(1meV/1TeV) was solved from this very equation, "E_c is of the same order as the dark-energy scale" is a restatement of anchor 2; using it to verify anchor 2 (ρ_Λ ~ (meV)⁴) is circular. Four-way verdict = semi-fitted. Still valid: the meV order of magnitude (independent of β sensitivity). See the round-53 block at the end of this section.

【v2.9 honest note】 E_c is extremely sensitive to β: β +1% ⟹ E_c = 2.068 meV (2.0× larger); β +2% ⟹ 4.14 meV (4.1×). Hence the meV order of magnitude is a robust conclusion, while the numerical value of E_c is entirely tied to β = 0.615 — the same root cause as the failure of the quantitative Ω_Λ route in §8.4 (dlnΩ_Λ/dβ = 465).

【v2.9 supplement (round 53: S-E5's ε is a scale misplacement, and E_c is an identity)】 Audited object = S-6 (the FQH graviton mode), the only ★★★ item on the list. No numerics were run: this round is arithmetic + citation checking (script _se5_eps_53.py, SE-1…SE-8, 7 pass / 0 fail).

· ① Scale misplacement (verdict: failure): §5.8 and §9 state ε ~ 10⁻²⁰ for S-E5, but that is this section's ε₀ = ε(M_Pl). Using this section's own law ε(E) = ε₀(M_Pl/E)^β, at FQH scales (GaAs 2DEG: ℏω_c = 86–259 meV, E_C = 9.7–16.9 meV, Δ_GMP ≈ 0.6E_C, k_BT@mK = 0.9–4.3 μeV) one gets ε ∈ [0.042, 77] ⟹ 18–21 orders of magnitude away from 10⁻²⁰. Line 892 of this section already states ε(1 meV) ~ 1 ⟹ §8.1 and S-E5 directly conflict (same symbol, two scales). · ② E_c is an identity (semi-fitted), not a【Derivation】: see the correction box above — E_c = 1TeV·ε(1TeV)^{1/β} = 1.000000 meV, M_Pl cancels, and "same order as the dark-energy scale" restates anchor 2 ⟹ circular. · ③ Γ_flux is undefined (not identifiable): the string Γ_flux occurs twice in the whole paper, both inside the same prediction sentence, with no expression. The observable is one real number (the linewidth Γ) ⟹ unknowns ≥ one free function ⟹ same disease as round 45 (axiom promotion) and round 52 (non-identifiable split) — the fourth occurrence. Criterion: unknowns > equations ⟹ not identifiable. · ④ Dilemma: the standard side already has a mechanism for the graviton mode's intrinsic decay rate and has extracted it (GMP 1970s → Haldane 2016 geometric theory → Liang et al., Nature 628, 78 (2024) experiment → Balram–Sreejith–Jain, PRL 133, 246605 (2024) splitting → Du, arXiv:2509.04408 (2025) effective theory → IOP Rep. Prog. Phys. (2025) decay-rate extraction; mechanism = decay into the two-magnetoroton continuum). If ε = O(1), then Γ_flux is of the same order as Γ_Coulomb ⟹ "framework-unique, absent in standard condensed matter" and "consistent with experiment" cannot both hold. · ⑤ Side finding: ε₀ had four variants (7.6 / 7.7 / 7.67 / 7.751 ×10⁻²⁰, a 2% spread); now unified to 7.731×10⁻²⁰. · Verdict: S-E5's ε ~ 10⁻²⁰ = failure; the【Derivation】label on E_c = semi-fitted; testability = not identifiable; "framework-unique" = not established. ⟹ both the ★★★ rating of S-6 and its "framework-unique" positioning must be downgraded; the list's claim of "the only ★★★ and unique item" does not hold. Repair paths: ① (mandatory) change S-E5's ε to ε(FQH scale) ≈ O(1) and "linewidth excess" to "an additional term of the same order as Γ_Coulomb"; ② (the only route to revival) supply an expression for Γ_flux and show it is an independent contribution relative to the standard Γ; ③ if neither, downgrade to "directional claim, no quantitative content".

8.2 Statistical decay factor (ε_B) and dark matter

【Derivation】 Planck-fluctuation raw density ρ_raw = 2m_P/l_P³ ≈ 10⁹⁷ kg/m³; statistical decay ε_B = (l_P/R_U)² ≈ 10⁻¹²⁰ (geometric origin, not (1/3)^n — n is actually 251); dark matter ρ_DM = ε_B·ρ_raw ≈ 1.6×10⁻²⁵ kg/m³ (differs from the observed 2.3×10⁻²⁷ by ~70×).

【v2.9 correction】 Under the holographic dilution ε_eff = (l_P/R_c)² = 1.3975×10⁻¹²², ⟨f_space⟩ is not a knob — it is uniquely solved from observations: ⟨f_space⟩ = ρ_DM/(ε_eff·ρ_P) = 2.2511×10⁻²⁷ / 7.2037×10⁻²⁶ = 0.031250073 = <strong>1/32.000</strong> (the earlier "≈0.014" came from a different ε_eff choice and is 2.23× too small; it is withdrawn). 【Honest note】 This relation is currently a fit, not a prediction: read as a void volume fraction it differs from the measured 50–80% by 16–26×; read as a rate-suppression factor it lacks any parameter-free suppression law f_space(ρ_m) (four self-consistent laws give four answers). ⟹ The quantitative explanation of dark matter remains a blank in this framework.

Topological origin of ε_B (H1–H4): quantification of "ε is the topologically forced 'minimal vortex-core density'"【Proposition chain H1–H4, based on Poincaré–Hopf】: - H1 (S² rigorous): χ(S²) = 2 ⟹ sum of zero indices of a tangent vector field = 2 ⟹ direction space has at least 2 vortex cores【Topological argument】; - H2 (vortex-core fraction): ε_topo(R) = (l_P/R)²/(2π) — probability that an event direction falls in a vortex-core cell; - H3 (component structure): ε_total = ε_topo + ε_A — topological dominance at Planck scale (0.159), dynamical dominance above TeV (ε_A); ε₀ = 7.67×10⁻²⁰ is the dynamical-component anchor; - H4 (significant finding): ε_B(cosmos) = 1.35×10⁻¹²³ ≈ ε_topo(cosmos)×2π — the dark-matter decay factor lies exactly at the order of the topologically forced minimum; the geometric origin of ε_B gains topological endorsement; - S³ clarification【Correction】: χ(S³)=0 and S³ is parallelizable (trivial tangent bundle); the hairy-ball theorem does not apply to 3D spatial slices (early-version correction) — vortex cores in spatial slices require Kakeya + leakage supplementation.

【v2.9 supplement (round 66: the birth of a soliton — topological residue under random annihilation)】 Triggering question: "in random annihilation, stable points that cannot be annihilated appear — that is the birth of matter". The framework-side counterparts are all pre-existing content: §2.3's hierarchy chain states "solitonization ⟹ particles (stable localized structures)"; §5.3 states "three fermion generations = the winding modes of vortices"; and H1 of this subsection supplies the topological forcing. This round turns it into a decidable problem (script _soliton_66.py, SO-1…SO-9, 9 pass / 0 fail).

① Numerical confirmation of H1 (icosphere subdiv 3 = 642 vertices; counted via the Morse spectrum): 30 independent random smooth fields all give Σ = #max − #saddle + #min ≡ 2 (identical in all 30 runs), while #max (mean 2.10), #min (2.07) and #saddle (2.17) differ every time. Three analytic calibrations (f = p_x / p_y / p_z, each with exactly 2 true critical points) all give +2 ✓ ⟹ the "at least 2 vortex cores" of H1 now has numerical support.

② Annihilation experiment: pairing randomly under "index +1 meeting −1 ⟹ both vanish", the endpoint distribution over 40 independent trials has a single outcome: 2 of +1 / 0 of −1. ⟹ random annihilation wipes out most vortex cores (50–98% depending on the configuration), but the net charge χ is a geometric identity — not one of them can be removed.

③ Counter-check (the hairy-ball theorem stated directly):

Surface χ Minimum number of vortex cores Admit a nowhere-zero field?
S² (direction space) 2 ≥ 2 No (hairy-ball theorem)
T² (torus) 0 0 Yes (v = ∂/∂u, modulus exactly 1.000000)
S³ 0 0 Yes (parallelizable)

⟹ "whether everything can be annihilated" is decided by χ, independently of randomness — the same criterion as the S³ clarification on line 1083 of this subsection.

④ H2 can be rewritten: ε_topo(R) = (l_P/R)²/(2π) = χ/(4π)·(l_P/R)² (since χ/(4π) = 1/(2π)) ⟺ "unit solid angle ΔΩ = (l_P/R)², with χ = 2 cores among the units". Substituting R = l_P: ε_topo(l_P) = 1/(2π) = 0.1591549 — exactly consistent with the H3 statement "Planck-scale topological dominance (0.159)" ✓ ⚠️ This is an arithmetic identity (recognizing 1/(2π) as χ/(4π)), not a new derivation.

⑤ The same sentence as the §5.7 string net: Σ index = χ = 2 (direction field) and Π_v A_v = Π_p B_p = 1 (string net) are two realizations of the same topological conservation law ⟹ registered as consistency, not as two independent results.

⚠️ Two methodological lessons (for later reuse): ① poles of a latitude–longitude grid degenerate the 1-ring — for vertex (1, j) the three "previous-row" neighbours all map to the same north pole ⟹ the winding numbers are all wrong (measured Σ = −8 instead of 2); switching to an icosphere fixes it. ② "the winding number of the normalized ∇f" ≠ "the index of a critical point" — normalization magnifies non-zero points with small |∇f| into spurious vortex cores (f = p_x reports 6 of +1, with artefacts sitting at |∇f| ≈ 0.156, which are not zeros at all) ⟹ topological counting must use the Morse spectrum. Relatedly, the criterion holds only on a smooth layer — a pointwise-independent random field gives Σ = +9 ≠ 2 ("everywhere is an extremum"), which is precisely the content of the "solitonization" step in §2.3.

⚠️ Three honest boundaries: ① this round computes static geometry; "annihilation" is an abstract pairing in the numerics, not derived from spacetime dynamics; ② ε leakage is not verified (P1 is a【framework assumption】) ⟹ topological charge ≠ matter–antimatter asymmetry; the two are independent propositions within the framework; ③ only topological irremovability was verified, not dynamical stability (which is a necessary but not sufficient condition for "a soliton becoming a particle"). ⚠️ Wording correction: it is not that "stable points appear in random fluctuations", but that they are topologically forced — the criterion being whether Σ is a random variable (it is not: 2 in all 30 runs).

【v2.9 supplement (round 67: soliton dynamics — vortex motion, annihilation, and a topological correction)】 Round 66 left open "annihilation is an abstract pairing; no dynamics was done". This round supplies it, and states its nature first: a citation sweep shows that "graph heat flow / Dirichlet energy / harmonic map / Laplace-Beltrami / parallel transport / connection / time evolution / evolution equation / gradient flow" occur 0 times each in this paper ⟹ the dynamics used here is a NEW assumption, not something the framework already had (the framework's own time evolution is only the §8.6.7 tick permutation; all four Hamiltonians are static). The only claim of compatibility: the tick is a permutation ⟹ it preserves topological charge, and the evolution used here also strictly preserves it. Scripts _vortex_67.py (VX-1…VX-9, 9 pass / 0 fail), _vortex_point_67.py (VP-1…VP-7, 7 pass / 0 fail).

① ⭐ The bare winding-number sum is not χ but 3χ (a numerical sharpening of H1 in this subsection): on icosphere(3) with geodesic parallel transport, the bare Σ w_i is identically +6 = 3χ (12 random axes × subdiv 2/3/4, spread 1.8×10⁻¹⁵); after subtracting curvature, Σ(w_i − 2K_i/2π) = χ = 2 (to 1e-15). The extra 2χ is exactly the discrete curvature: Gauss–Bonnet gives ΣK_i = 2πχ (the panel measures ΣK_i = 4π) ⟹ bare Σ = χ + 2χ = 3χ. ⚠️ Scope (further corrected in round 67): the corrected formula holds only for fields that are smooth AND whose zeros do not fall on lattice vertices. ⚠️ But the numbers that look like "counterexamples" (for ∇p_z the correction gives −2, for x̂×p it gives 0) are not counterexamples — the criterion is simply undefined at those points: measured, tilting the axis by 1e-9 rad brings ∇p_x/p_y/p_z all back to +6 / corrected +2 = χ, and replacing the zero vector by a random unit direction also returns to +6/+2 in 3/3 runs. The root cause is that "keep the zero vector + atan2(0,0) ≡ 0" is equivalent to artificially assigning that point's direction to the local E1, which breaks rotational covariance (icosahedral symmetry maps ±x̂ to ±ŷ, which ought to have equal values — measured 6 ≠ 4). ⟹ numbers at degenerate points can serve neither as counterexamples nor as supporting evidence; they are merely undefined. Also note: the minimum angular distance from a zero to the nearest vertex over the 12 random axes is 1.46° (never zero) ⟹ the calibration above is field-independent within the class of fields whose zeros avoid vertices (an explicitly selective calibration, stated as such). ⚠️ Same family as round 66: "discrete winding number" is by itself not a topological invariant unless corrected.

② On whether the winding number may be used at all — the correct relation is "the bare winding number is not χ", and each round needs its own alignment (⚠️ corrected in round 67: the earlier phrasing "legal for tangent fields, illegal for ∇f" was an inaccurate simplification and is withdrawn): round 66's artefacts came from the modulus of ∇f being arbitrarily small away from zeros (it is not a normalized field), with normalizing magnifying "small modulus" into "wildly turning direction" at a scale comparable to the 1-ring; here v is itself a unit tangent vector field (modulus identically 1, so that magnification mechanism is absent), yet its bare winding-number sum is still 3χ rather than χ (see ①). ⟹ the two rounds' conclusions are two halves of one sentence: the bare winding number is not χ. Round 66 needs "switch to the Morse spectrum", this round needs "subtract the discrete curvature"; only after each is aligned does χ come out.

②b Diagnosing line 1105 (new in round 67; answering "does the main text violate its own prescription?"): ∇f is both the gradient of a scalar field and a tangent vector field, so both routes above can assign it a number — this round ran exactly that bridging experiment (_morse_vs_winding_67.py, MW-1…MW-3, 3 pass / 0 fail, with MW-1 being a refutation item): · Morse spectrum: 12/12 all give 2 (it counts the type of critical point and does not depend on how fast the field turns, so it is robust) · Curvature-corrected winding number: 18/20 give 2 (VX-1f, 20 smooth random scalar fields), while the bare winding number never gives χ (values in [5.0, 6.0]). ⚠️ The two deviations are not systematic error: re-mounting the same continuous field on a finer mesh (subdiv 4, 642 → 2562 vertices) restores seed 7/14 to 2, while seed 0 goes the other way (2 at subdiv 3, 1 at subdiv 4) ⟹ the failure is always ±1 unit and migrates with resolution = a mesh-to-field alignment coincidence (my MW-1 and verify-agent's VX-1f are two independent implementations reaching the same conclusion). ⚠️ I first guessed "failure = zero too close to a vertex" (MW-1: the failing case sits at 3.60° while passing cases sit at 0.77°/1.44°/1.95° — uncorrelated), then "core spacing / minimal edge length" (VX-1f: that quantity holds on all 20 seeds and is therefore not a discriminator) ⟹ both hypotheses were refuted by measurement. ⚠️ The Morse spectrum itself also requires the smoothness precondition: without it (pointwise independent noise) Morse Σ ∈ {11, 12, 14, 15} ≠ χ. ⟹ The two routes are NOT "equivalent" — what we have is an empirical agreement rate of 18/20 plus one counter-example in the opposite direction; writing "equivalent" would be an assertion holding for arbitrary fields and would exceed the evidence. The correct strength is: "they agree on the common domain of validity (smooth, zeros off the lattice) in 18/20 cases, and the Morse spectrum is more robust under resolution refinement (20/20)". ⚠️ By this project's own rule, empirical agreement must not be upgraded to a theorem; and the 18/20 plus that one counter-example are positive information — they show the discrepancy comes from discretization alignment, not from the counting logic itself. The sole reason round 67 uses the curvature-corrected winding number is that it can be computed vertex-by-vertex at every instant of v_t, whereas the Morse spectrum requires the whole field and cannot be attached to a step-by-step evolving v_t. This is not a classification of "which object is legal" but of "which one can be hung onto the dynamics". ⚠️ This round obtained a "discrete incidental failure" from two independent routes: the ∇f route fails 1/12; the tangent-field route is "strictly conserved step-by-step in 0/40". ⟹ This is the common price of discrete topological counting (a methodological note).

③ The dynamics really does make vortices annihilate by themselves (evolution = the graph heat flow inside the tangent bundle, v ← (1−α)v + α·⟨v'⟩, α = 0.3, not a sampled pairing):

Initial state Vortex count (t = 0 / 20 / 50 / 100 / 200)
noisy layer 299 → 72 → 40 → 22 → 6 (−98%)
smooth layer 18 → 18 → … → 6 (t ≈ 1000)
T² (χ = 0) 130 → 12 → 0 (maximum absolute value of Σ = 1.3×10⁻¹⁵, strictly zero)

⭐ 5 seeds × 2 initial layers × 2000 steps all converge to the same 6 (+1) vortices — independent of initial state, seed and duration. The point-particle layer (overdamped vortices, dr_i/dt = q_i Σ_j q_j (r_i−r_j)/|r_i−r_j|²) gives an analytic scaling: the meeting time of a (+1,−1) pair is t* = r₀²/4 (five cases, relative error < 0.03%), with d(r²)/dt = −4 for opposite and +4 for like signs (fully symmetric); under continuous noise generation the net charge stays exactly Δ. ⚠️ This takes the same scaling as §4.2's R_mix = l_P√(t/t_P) (R² ∝ t) but from a different source (diffusion equation vs overdamped vortex motion) ⟹ no claim of deep unification.

④ Two rounds independently reach the same conclusion: topological charge is defined only on the smooth layer: round 66 (a pointwise-independent random field gives Σ = +9 ≠ 2); round 67 (the noisy layer's bare Σ ∈ [−5, +4], over 40 scans mean +0.35 / std 2.40 — no topological charge at all; the smooth layer has it). ⟹ the "solitonization" step of §2.3 is necessary; ⚠️ but its mechanism is still not derived from first principles this round (only its necessity was verified).

⑤ This does not constitute a third result: line 1103 of this subsection already registers "the direction field's Σ index = χ" and "the string net's Π_v A_v = 1" as two realizations of the same topological conservation law, explicitly stating they are "not two independent results" ⟹ this round merely adds dynamics to that same conservation law and adds nothing to the count.

⚠️ Six honest boundaries: ① the dynamics is a new assumption (0 hits in the sweep); ② the bare winding number is 3χ and the correction has a scope; ③ conservation is "topological-quantum-level approximate" — Σw is an exact integer (40/40) but is strictly constant step-by-step in 0/40, has spread ≤ 2 in 39/40 and mode = final value in 39/40 (when a vortex hops across an edge the discrete winding number reads ±1, occasionally ±2, and recovers on the next step — not a drift); moreover "strictly constant step-by-step" and "vortex count decreasing" are mutually exclusive (both hold in 0/40); ④ seed-independence is a product of the evolution (at t = 0, 2 of 5 seeds read 5); ⑤ the noisy layer has no topological charge; ⑥ stable ≠ dynamically stable — what is proved is "topologically irremovable" (necessary), not "robust against arbitrary perturbations" (sufficient).

【v2.9 supplement (round 70: the coarse-graining cutoff and the mass of Φ — why the two gaps cannot share one scale)】 This round merges I-18 (round 67) with I-17 (round 62). Reason for merging: §4.5b states that Φ's mathematical identity is the spectral action S = Tr f(D²/Λ²) (which carries a cutoff Λ), while line 423 gives a group-theoretic definition of "coarse-graining" ⟹ if the two shared one and the same cutoff, they would be one mechanism — and that is decidable. Script _coarsegrain_70.py (CG-1…CG-10, 11 pass / 0 fail).

① Ten implementations of "coarse-graining / cutoff" in the framework (with their calibers): ① group-theoretic coarse-graining (line 423, "the orbit partition of G is the maximal tick-invariant coarse-graining", no scale) | ② the source of macroscopic irreversibility (line 1317, "the bottom layer produces no entropy, so macroscopic irreversibility can only come from coarse-graining") | ③ Planck cutoff (line 322, "cutoff = Planck (given naturally by axiom 1)", scale l_P) | ④ spectral-action cutoff Λ (line 791, Λ is not given) | ⑤ F2 down-scaling (line 311, δ = √(R·l_P)) | ⑥ Q_res (line 674, named "coarse-graining cutoff scale" in round 56) | ⑦ probability-normalization scale E_c (line 1090, an identity, C-4) | ⑧ the μ of the Φ⁴ potential (line 984, μ is an input) | ⑨ rigidity cutoff r_c = GM/c² (line 1585) | ⑩ Boltzmann weak-field cutoff (lines 187/261). ⟹ Only ④ and ⑧ are pure inputs — and precisely these two fix the mass of Φ.

② The criterion: the two are structurally different — group-theoretic coarse-graining uses only a permutation group and contains no dimensional quantity whatsoever (the implementation _tickgroup.gens_correct contains 0 floating-point literals), whereas every cutoff must carry a scale (R, 1 meV, GM/c², l_P) ⟹ one is combinatorial, the other metric; they cannot be the same thing.

③ The answer to I-18: the smooth layer is not produced by the substrate — starting from a maximally smooth initial state (z ∈ {0,1,2} fully occupied) and running ticks ((2,2,6), z-line length 6): the block-density variance B decays from 0.250000 to 0.006944 within 2 steps (random baseline 0.020833); within a single tick orbit the spread of B is 0.250000, that of the nearest-neighbour equal-value fraction S is 0.590909, and |d₀ − d₁| falls from 1.0000 to 0.0000 ⟹ tick evolution destroys smoothness; it does not create it. ⟹ "Smooth" = "read on a scale larger than l_P" (an interpretation of this round, not the framework's own wording) — compatible with line 322 ("cutoff = Planck") and with B5's observer resolution; the Gaussian kernel σ = 0.55 rad used in rounds 66/67 is an external choice of the toy model.

④ I-17 reinforced, plus a new discriminable structure: the mass of Φ hangs on the two pure inputs ④ and ⑧ ⟹ the postulate shift is not resolved. ⭐ But inverting the F2 down-scaling law (E(R) = √(ℏc·E_P/R), all in eV with ℏc = 1.97327×10⁻⁷ eV·m) gives a correspondence between mass tier and force range:

m_Φ taken as force range λ = ℏc/m inside the fifth-force search window (10⁻⁶ → 3.1×10²⁰ m)?
string-net scalar sector Δ_scalar = 0.41 J 0.813 l_P ❌ off by 28.9 orders of magnitude
coarse-graining cutoff @ E_c (1.0181 meV) 0.1938 mm ✅
coarse-graining cutoff @ E(R_H) (4.1894 meV) 0.0471 mm ✅
Planck (axiom 1) 1.000 l_P ❌ off by 28.8 orders of magnitude

⟹ The range ratio between (a) and (b) is 1.475×10³¹, i.e. 31.2 orders of magnitude — whether a fifth force can be seen depends on which tier Φ's mass takes. ⚠️ But being inside the window is only a necessary condition (a scalar force of gravitational strength at 0.05–0.2 mm has long been excluded by Eöt-Wash-type experiments; only a very weakly coupled narrow band survives), and the coupling strength is precisely the other half that I-17 does not give ⟹ (b)/(c) are still not testable predictions.

⑤ A by-product (inversion): E(R) = 1 meV ⟹ R = 16.93 R_H (cross-check: (4.19/1.0181)² = 16.93 ✓). ⚠️ But 1 meV is an identity (C-4), and 4.19 meV is the same combination as the standard dark-energy scale estimate √(M_Pl H₀) (C-6) ⟹ neither meV is a new result.

⚠️ Seven honest boundaries: ① "m_Φ = Λ" is an assumption (four-way verdict = half-fitting); the framework has never tied Φ's mass to any concrete scale; ② "smooth = read on a scale larger than l_P" is this round's interpretation, not the framework's own statement; ③ (2,2,6) is still a small lattice, with no L ≥ 8 extrapolation; ④ this round introduces no new evolution equation (it uses the framework's own tick — unlike round 67); ⑤ being inside the range window ≠ being observable; ⑥ this round's "31.2 orders of magnitude" is the ratio between two candidates, whereas round 62's "28.9–55.4" is the distance from a candidate to the window — different calibers, do not mix; ⑦ implementation-level finding (must be recorded): at (2,2,4) the z-line length 4 makes "block variance" degenerate into a tick invariant (the phase-1 matching (1 2)(3 0) happens to swap the two blocks carved out by the z-pairing as a whole ⟹ (d₀ − 0.5)² is invariant, measured spread = 0) ⟹ any future criterion using "block variance" must avoid z-line length 4.

8.3 Dark-matter superposition model

【Derivation】Dark matter = this-branch Planck-fluctuation remnants (ε_B mechanism) + mirror-branch gravitational projection (cross-branch mechanism):

ρDM = εB ρraw fspace + κmirror ρmirror
  • Mirror-branch projection: 【v2.9 correction: falsified】 the earlier "same order as observation" claim fails. The two natural readings of 50:50 give opposite-direction discrepancies: reading A (only gravity shared across branches, the rest filtered ⟹ Ω_dm = Ω_b = 0.0493) is 5.31× too low; reading B (keeping κ_extra ≈ Ω_mirror/Ω_total ≈ 0.5 ⟹ Ω_dm ~ 1) is 3.82× too high — both far outside the error bar. ⟹ The claim that the mirror branch supplies the bulk of dark matter is withdrawn; this subsection is downgraded to an open problem, and the void-CPT-κ anomaly holds only if the mechanism is rebuilt.
  • Testable prediction: void-CPT-κ anomaly (void regions with stronger weak lensing + mass–light decoupling, most prominent at 10–30 Mpc/h scales; candidate objects CDG-2, J0613+52).

8.4 Dark energy and w(z)

【Derivation】 Dark energy = contribution of the ε_A saturation region ρ_Λ ~ (E_c)⁴; the saturation scale drifts slowly with cosmic expansion E_c(z) = E_c,0(1+z)^ε ⟹

w = -1 + 4ε3 = -1 + 2ν

Observational constraint (DESI 2024+CMB: w = −1.028±0.031): |ε| < 0.023, |ν| < 0.0155. The framework predicts w = const (wa=0) — directly testable with DESI DR2/Euclid. ⚠️【Round-73 discriminating-power annotation】 This "prediction" is not framework-specific — ΛCDM also gives w_a = 0 (w = −1 exactly ⟹ w_a = 0); the framework merely relaxes w₀ = −1 to w₀ = −1 + 2ν (ν free) ⟹ ΛCDM is the framework's special case at ν = 0. Hence the 3.3σ pressure mentioned below is borne jointly by the framework and ΛCDM and must not be read as "the framework's dark-energy prediction is being discriminated by existing data"; the only genuine discriminating opportunity is "can the framework uniquely fix ν ≠ 0?" — currently it cannot (see the round-73 block in §8.6.5). 【v2.7 supplement: DESI DR2 (2025-03) discrimination status (referee M5)】: DESI DR2+CMB exclude ΛCDM at 3.1σ (+Pantheon+ 2.8σ / +Union3 3.8σ / +DESY5 4.2σ), with CPL best fit w₀≈−0.76±0.06, wa≈−0.77±0.23 — exerting ~3.3σ pressure on the framework's wa=0 prediction (the framework's dark-energy prediction is being discriminated by existing data: if the DDE signal is confirmed ⟹ this prediction is falsified; if a systematic effect ⟹ the framework survives). Verdict timeline: DESI final analysis 2026-04 + Euclid first cosmological data 2026-10 — final verdict within 0-2 years. Honest note: the DDE-vs-systematics debate is unresolved (ACT DR6/SPT-3G CMB support ΛCDM); the w₀ discrimination is a cross-model wCDM/CPL comparison (not directly comparable; the wa=0 test is authoritative).

8.6 Horizon thermodynamics and flatness

Notation (important): this section does not use λ for the density ratio — λ is already taken in §5 by the string-net Hamiltonian coupling and its critical value λ_c ≈ 0.14 (11 lines in §5, 44 in the whole (measured in round 72; the earlier 26 is stale) paper; §8 had none). We use the standard symbol Ω_tot ≡ ρ/ρ_crit, with Ω_K = 1 − Ω_tot. A useful side effect: the earlier note "λ = 1" is revealed to be Ω_tot = 1 — i.e. flatness itself. ⚠️ Round-72 addendum (C-15): λ carries a third meaning — the Φ⁴ quartic coupling of §6.4, V(Φ) = −½μ²Φ² + ¼λΦ⁴ (not mentioned in this convention before); and g carries two meanings under one letter (§6.1's pinning coupling gΦψ̄ψ vs §5.7's string-net geometric factor J = n_G·E_P·g), previously undeclared. ⚠️ Round-75 addendum (C-21): ρ carries at least 5 meanings — ρ_pair (event-rate density, §2.2) / ρ_raw (primordial fluctuation density, §8.2) / the equipartition ρ (mass density, §8.6.3 here) / ρ_Λ / ρ_DM; and w carries at least 2 — §8.4's w = −1 + 4ε/3 (dark-energy component, from E_c drift) versus round 75's w = −1/3 (the total on the surface, from conservation of E ∝ 1/H). ⟹ always state which one is meant (same family as C-15 / C-16; the two do not contradict each other, being different objects).

8.6.1 The thermodynamic surface = the advection-stall surface

【Derivation】 The microscopic constituents propagate at c. In uniform expansion the relation v_rec = Hr holds exactly and independently of k, so the interface beyond which storm components cannot be advected back lies at

Rc = c/H0 = 14.4516 Gly (H0 = 2.1927×10-18 s^{-1})

Taking κ = c²/R_c = cH₀ and the Unruh temperature gives

kBT = ℏ H02π ⟹ T = 2.66558×10-30 K

【Assumption B5 (quasi-theorem)】 The thermodynamic surface is the advection-stall surface above. It is neither the GR apparent horizon nor the event horizon; see 8.6.5 for the discrimination.

【Honest note】 The surface is not a null surface: dR_c/dt = (1+q₀)c, and q₀ = −0.5333 for ΛCDM ⟹ it moves outward at 0.4667c (timelike); it is null only in the pure de Sitter (w = −1) limit. It must therefore not be described as "the boundary of the observer's causal patch." The correct supporting argument is the timescale separation of 8.6.4.

8.6.2 β = 1/(2π) and the origin of 2π

【Derivation】 The mean energy per Planck cell l_P² is k_BT = ℏH₀/(2π), i.e.

β ≡ energy per bitkBTGH = 12π

This coefficient is independent of Ω_tot. Its origin traces back to the F3 entropy formula: combining S = k_BA/(4l_P²) with dE = TdS gives T = ℏc³/(8πGMk_B), which yields 6.1687×10⁻⁸ K for a solar-mass black hole, within 10⁻⁹ of the standard Hawking temperature. 1/(2π) therefore originates in the "4" of F3 — it is not borrowed from QFT.

【Honest note】 The coefficient 1/4 in S = k_BA/(4l_P²) is the Bekenstein–Hawking value (independently verified by the solar-black-hole entropy S/k_B = 1.0494×10⁷⁷); it is standard-physics input, not a framework derivation.

8.6.3 Equipartition ⟹ the flat Friedmann constraint

【Derivation】 Writing the algebraic equipartition E = Nk_BT on this surface, with N = A/(4l_P²) = πR_c²/l_P²:

4π3ρ(cH0)3 c2 = π(c/H0)2lP2·ℏ H02π ⟹ ρ = 3H028π G

⚠️ 【Round-75 notation note (C-20): the area convention must be stated】 The left side uses the full-sphere volume (4π/3)R³, while the right side takes N = πR²/l_P² (projected/cross-sectional area), not 4πR²/l_P². Taking the full-sphere area gives ρ = 3H²/(2πG) — a factor of 4.000 off (3.4395×10⁻²⁶ vs 8.5987×10⁻²⁷ kg/m³). ⚠️ This projection is the same one as §4.9's B5 ("bulk = surface projection", I = H(Σ)) ⟹ the convention is self-consistent, but was not previously stated.

【v2.9 supplement (round 40: the status of equipartition)】 This round supplies the mechanism-level account of the equipartition ledger: · The uniform distribution (= equipartition) is the unique invariant measure of the tick dynamics on each orbit — numerically, applying any tick to the uniform distribution leaves it invariant term by term ⟹ equipartition needs no dynamical thermalisation; · but there are many orbits (1357 on 2×2×4, see the round-40 block in §4.8) ⟹ the dynamics preserves orbits and cannot bring the system to equipartition ⟹ equipartition is structural / an initial condition, consistent with rounds 21 / 26; this round supplies the mechanism-level reason (the existence of invariants). ⚠️ Extrapolation not verified: the above is exhaustive only for N ≤ 16.

All inputs are definitions or previously derived framework results; Friedmann is not presupposed. Numerically ρ = 8.5983×10⁻²⁷ kg/m³.

⟹ Ω_tot = 1, i.e. Ω_K = 0, i.e. k = 0; and

M = c³/(2GH₀) = 9.20550×10⁵² kg ;  R_S = 2GM/c² = R_c (exact, relative error <10⁻¹²)

Hence the Hubble sphere equals the Schwarzschild radius: the "black-hole universe" acquires a mechanism hereand is no longer a numerical coincidence.

⚠️ 【Round-73 annotation (C-17): this is an identity, not a mechanism】 Substituting back: R_S = 2G·[c³/(2GH₀)]/c² = c/H₀ ≡ R_c — both G and H₀ cancel, identically for any H₀ (numerically checked at three arbitrary H₀: relative differences 0.0e+00 / 2.3e−16 / 0.0e+00). ⟹ It is not "a coincidence explained", but something weaker than a coincidence: an identity with zero content (the same disease as E_c ≡ 1 meV (C-4) in round 53: substitute the intermediate parameters back and nothing remains). Only the equipartition account itself carries content (ρ = 3H²/8πG, which does not assume Friedmann). and is no longer a numerical coincidence.

【Honest note】 M uses the Misner–Sharp mass expression, i.e. GR is used inside FRW ⟹ strictly this is "deriving k = 0 within FRW", not deriving gravity from scratch. Also ρ = 3H²/8πG is only the constraint equation; the acceleration and continuity equations are still missing (they are equivalent given the constraint).

8.6.4 Why the two ends count the same coherent blocks (timescale separation)

【Preliminary】 The coherent-block unit is the same on both ends: 1 nat per 4l_P², not 1 bit (decided by the Hawking temperature: 1 bit would give 8.8995×10⁻⁸ K, a +44.27% deviation). The remaining question is whether the two ends count the same blocks.

【Derivation】 The block decomposition is instantaneous — defined by the storm's current microstructure, not by history. The storm refreshes at ν = 1/t_P = 1.85486×10⁴³ s⁻¹ while the horizon geometry changes only at H₀:

νH0 = RclP = 8.45924×1060, H0tP = 1.18214×10-61

Each cell is refreshed 8.46×10⁶⁰ times per Hubble time, so the difference between the two decompositions is bounded by O(H₀t_P) = 1.18×10⁻⁶¹.

⟹ The "same blocks" statement holds to O(10⁻⁶¹). Control case: if ν → 0 (frozen microstructure), the mismatch is O(1+q₀) = 0.47, i.e. a 20%–47% error in the block count — the A1 postulate ν ~ 1/t_P is precisely what makes this link work.

【Honest note】 This is a bounding argument, not a constructive proof; it rests on two unproven premises: (i) both counts are instantaneous (it fails if either end accumulates history); (ii) the refresh rate is 1/t_P.
